Which apps can become agent toolkits?

An agent researched 100 requested apps for Composio: auth, self-serve vs gated access, API surface, MCP, and a buildability verdict, all backed by quoted evidence. The results were then checked by code, by a second critic agent, against Composio's own catalog, and by a blind human audit. Researched 2026-11-23 · source repo · records.json · records.csv · summary.md

Headline findings

  1. 58 of 100 apps can be built as agent toolkits today: documented API, and a developer can get credentials free or on a trial.
  2. Auth is not the bottleneck; access is. 72/100 offer OAuth2, 83/100 offer an API key or personal token, and 58 offer both. The real blockers are commercial: paid tier or API add-on (16), vendor app approval (10), partner / sales gate (6).
  3. Self-serve varies by category. Most open: Developer, Infra and Data platforms (10/10), CRM and Sales (9/10). Most gated: Finance and Fintech (6/10), AI, Research and Media-native (7/10).
  4. 74/100 already exist in Composio's catalog. Of the 26 that don't, 11 are "build now": the easiest new toolkits (LiveAgent, Vonage, GoHighLevel, systeme.io, WooCommerce, Amazon Selling Partner, …).
  5. 6 need a sales or partner conversation before any developer access (DealCloud, Gladly, Aircall, Salesforce Commerce Cloud, Waterfall.io, PitchBook). 85/100 already ship an official MCP server.

Self-serve = a developer can get credentials free or on a trial. "API on all plans" = the API works on any customer plan (a paid product with the API on every plan counts as yes).

E.g. HubSpot Marketplace listing, Attio's 25-workspace cap for unpublished apps. This doesn't block building, but Composio's shared OAuth app needs vendor approval to scale.

Priorities

Easy wins not yet in Composio (11)

Verdict "build now" and no matching toolkit in Composio's catalog of 1842 toolkits.

  • LiveAgent Support and Helpdesk · broad API · MCP official
  • Vonage Communications and Messaging · broad API · MCP official
  • GoHighLevel Marketing, Ads, Email and Social · broad API · MCP official
  • systeme.io Marketing, Ads, Email and Social · broad API · MCP official
  • WooCommerce Ecommerce · broad API · MCP official
  • Amazon Selling Partner Ecommerce · broad API · MCP official
  • SE Ranking Data, SEO and Scraping · broad API · MCP official
  • Netlify Developer, Infra and Data platforms · broad API · MCP official
  • MongoDB Atlas Developer, Infra and Data platforms · broad API · MCP official
  • Binance Finance and Fintech · broad API · MCP official
  • Reducto AI, Research and Media-native · moderate API · MCP official

Needs outreach (6)

Sales/partner gate before any developer access.

  • DealCloud CRM and Sales
    No self-serve developer access. Getting credentials needs a DealCloud site, so a developer needs a customer tenant or a sandbox from Intapp. Sandboxes are offered to Intapp clients. Each customer must also have an admin enable the API capability and issue a per-user API key. Whether the API is a separately licensed add-on is unconfirmed.
  • Gladly Support and Helpdesk
    No self-serve or trial developer access was found. Credentials require an existing Gladly customer org with the API User permission, and a sandbox must be purchased through a Gladly CSM or Support. A customer or Gladly contact is needed to start.
  • Aircall Communications and Messaging
    A multi-tenant OAuth integration needs approval as an Aircall Technology Partner, which Aircall can decline. Aircall issues the sandbox and the OAuth credentials only after approval. Basic Auth keys work now but only for a single customer's own account.
  • Salesforce Commerce Cloud Ecommerce
    No self-serve or free developer route to a B2C Commerce instance. Dev credentials and sandboxes require a licensed tenant and purchased sandbox credits through a Salesforce Account Executive. Each customer's Account Manager admin must then create an API client or SLAS client. No central OAuth app exists.
  • Waterfall.io Data, SEO and Scraping
    API keys are not self-serve; developers must contact the Waterfall team for access and pricing before they can get credentials.
  • PitchBook Finance and Fintech
    There is no self-serve developer access. The API needs a standalone contract with PitchBook's Direct Data team, an API key from PitchBook, a sandbox key from the account manager, and purchased credits. MCP access also needs a PitchBook license or a request to PitchBook.

Buildable with a named condition (32)

paid tier or API add-on (16): Salesforce, Copper, Freshdesk, Pylon, Help Scout, Lark (Larksuite), SendGrid, Squarespace, Ecwid, Ahrefs, Smartsheet, iPayX, Xero, NotebookLM, Otter AI, higgsfield

vendor app approval (10): Intercom, Slack, Discord, Google Ads, Meta Ads, LinkedIn Ads, Pinterest, Threads (Meta), Plaid, QuickBooks

none (1): Shopify

org admin must enable (3): Magento (Adobe Commerce), fanbasis, Ramp

unclear: needs research (2): Brex, Devin

Limited or unclear (4)

  • Sherlock Data, SEO and Scraping
    unclear: needs research: Sherlock is a local CLI with no hosted API and no credentials. A Composio toolkit would need to run it in a sandbox or container. Whether that execution model is supported is unresolved.
  • Datadog Developer, Infra and Data platforms
    unclear: needs research: Free-tier API access is unconfirmed. OAuth2 is restricted to approved Technology Partners, so general toolkits would rely on API key + application key or PATs.
  • Paygent Connect Finance and Fintech
    unclear: needs research: There is no public API reference, auth documentation or self-serve developer signup for the Pathfinder/Connect API. Credentials may require merchant underwriting or a sales/partner conversation.
  • Mermaid CLI AI, Research and Media-native
    restricted / local-only tool: It is only a local CLI with narrow scope (render diagrams to files). It has no remote or hosted API and needs Node.js and headless Chromium at runtime, so it could only work as a sandboxed local-execution tool.

Score history. First scored 2026-11-24 02:27 UTC, when some flagged apps had not been re-researched yet (usage limit). After npm run finish completed them, re-scored 2026-11-24 02:36 UTC. The audited scores did not change. Grading rules were not changed between the two.

C correct, I incorrect, U unresolved (agent or human said unknown). "Correct of all" counts unknowns against the agent. Calibration apps could guide fixes; holdout apps were only scored. Grading rules: auth = same set (api_key ≡ personal_token); access = dev and production both match; API = same core type and breadth within one step; MCP and verdict = exact. Sample seed 20261123, pilot apps excluded. 20 apps is a sample: these rates describe the sample, not an exact accuracy for all 100.

Why the remaining 24 final fields disagree with the human

Every field graded I (incorrect) in the FINAL version, grouped by cause. Grouping is a judgement made after scoring, by reading the agent record next to the human row. Scores themselves were NOT changed.

CauseFieldsCasesWhat it means
Paid product vs paid API (definition)9Gorgias: access, verdict
Vonage: access, verdict
Snowflake: access, verdict
Sentry: access, verdict
Gladly: access		Human marked production access as paid_plan because the product is paid; the agent followed the rubric rule 'API included on every plan = not an API gate'. A rubric-definition gap, not a factual error by either side.
Agent missed an auth method4Vonage: OAuth2
Zoho Cliq: API key
Bright Data: Basic
Snowflake: other		Incomplete auth lists, i.e. the agent stopped after finding the main method.
MCP classification3Gladly: community (agent) vs official (human)
fanbasis: official vs announced
NotebookLM: community vs none found		Official vs community vs announced is hard to establish from search results.
Gate type3Pinterest: access, verdict (app review vs outreach)
Squarespace: production app_review vs paid_plan		Which kind of gate applies (app review vs outreach vs paid).
API surface2Gladly: CLI missed
fanbasis: breadth broad vs moderate		Types or breadth.
Human listed two values2Harvest: access
Xero: access		Human wrote 'free;free_trial'; the strict grader needs one exact value. Agent value was one of the two.
Verdict judgement1Fathom: build now (agent) vs limited (human)Same facts, different overall call.

Takeaway: the agent does not invent facts (all quotes verified). Its remaining errors are incomplete lists (a missed second auth method) and classification judgement. The biggest single source of disagreement is a definition (is a paid product a "paid plan" gate?), which a research process at Composio's scale must pin down in the rubric before automating.

Salesforce also supports the JWT bearer flow and client credentials flow, which are widely used for server-to-server integrations (service_account_or_jwt). The detail text says these 'were not verified', so the methods list is probably incomplete. Check the JWT bearer and client credentials flows.

Google Adsaccess.production_accessfreeapp_reviewL3[L3] verdict: suspect: build_now looks too generous. Production use of a multi-tenant toolkit needs Explorer access, which Google 'may let you apply for' after initial sign-up, so it is not guaranteed. Explorer is capped at 2,880 ops/day and excludes account linking, user management and keyword planning. Real scale needs Standard, which is a manual audit of about 10 business days. The record also lists known issues with rejected or unhonored upgrades. This looks like build_with_conditions (access-tier application, with a Standard audit for scale), so a researcher should check it.

Under the rubric a paid product is not an API gate, so the main gate is no self-serve dev access, not paid_plan. Check whether a partner or trial sandbox route exists (Salesforce Partner Program, Trailhead, or a free developer sandbox) and how dev_access should be labelled. Evidence on this was not found.
Salesforce Commerce Cloudverdictbuild_with_conditionsoutreach_requiredL3[L3] access: suspect: dev_access=paid_plan is plausibly right, since on-demand sandboxes need purchased credits and the demo sandbox is read-only shopper only. But production_access=free is awkward: the API is included at no extra cost for existing customers, yet the platform needs a B2C Commerce licence. Under the rubric a paid product is not an API gate, so the main gate is no self-serve dev access, not paid_plan. Check whether a partner or trial sandbox route exists (Salesforce Partner Program, Trailhead, or a free developer sandbox) and how dev_access should be labelled. Evidence on this was not found.

Honest limits

  • Docs-checked, not call-tested. No authenticated API call was made to any of the 100 apps. Findings describe what the official docs say.
  • The quote check proves quotes are real, not that they're interpreted right. All 721 quotes matched their pages, so the agent never invented a quote. Errors come from missing conditions and judgment, which is why the critic and the human audit exist.
  • My own rule had false alarms. The L1 "official MCP must be on the vendor's domain" rule flagged vendor-owned alternate domains (e.g. sentry.dev, helpscout.net). Flags are prompts for re-checking, not automatic edits.
  • Composio's catalog is a partial oracle. It lists the auth Composio implements, which can be a subset of what the vendor offers. We check "Composio's methods ⊆ ours", not equality.
  • Usage limits shaped the run. On a Claude Pro subscription the first pass hit the 5-hour limit at app 60 and resumed after the reset. The re-research pass (L5) ran the 20 audited apps first, then the rest by id.
  • The improvement is modest and measured honestly. The loop raised correct fields by 6 points in both the calibration and the untouched holdout group. The scores were computed once with fixed grading rules and not adjusted afterwards.
  • The critic is also an LLM. Its "suspect" notes come from model knowledge and are only acted on through re-research with sources. The human audit is the only ground truth.

The agent

InputApp name + hint (100 from the brief, or any new app)
Research loopClaude (Sonnet) via Claude Agent SDK decides what to search and read next, until the evidence is enough
Tools via Composioweb_search / fetch_page run through Composio's SDK (Keenable toolkit), served to the agent as MCP tools. Each page is cached for verification.
Structured recordSchema-validated JSON; every claim = URL + verbatim quote
Verification L1–L5Code checks, critic agent, Composio catalog, re-research
PageGenerated from saved data only

Where a human was needed

  • Rubric decisions. The pilot showed "customer admin consent" and "vendor app review" being treated as gates. A human ruled: admin consent is normal; app review is tracked separately. A paid product ≠ a paid API.
  • Freezing the baseline. The prompt was fixed after a 6-app pilot, then frozen for all 100 so the before/after comparison is fair.
  • Blind audit. 20 apps checked by hand from official docs before seeing agent output; this is the accuracy ground truth.
  • Tool choice. Composio search/fetch toolkits that need a paid account (Tavily, Firecrawl, Exa) were skipped; Keenable's public toolkit works with no extra signup.
  • Usage limits. Resuming after the subscription reset.

Run stats

  • Model: claude-sonnet-5-5 (research + critic) · prompt 41807f85
  • Average per app: 38s, 5.9 tool calls, max 12 searches / 14 fetches
  • Usage: ~$38 notional for the first pass (Claude Pro subscription, not billed per call)
  • Records failed: 0 (failed attempts are retried on resume)
  • Run id baseline-v1: all raw data is in runs/baseline-v1/ in the repo

All 100 apps + 1 added later

Apps added after the study (Calendly) went through the same agent and automatic checks (rules, quote check, critic). They are not human audited and are not counted in any number above. Filter them with the category "Scheduling".

1Salesforce
CRM and Sales
 oauth2 service_account_or_jwt free
prod: paid_plan
 rest, soap, sdk, cli, webhooks, graphql
broad
 official ✓ salesforce Build with conditions
The API needs an edition that includes it (Enterprise or above, or the Professional add-on). Developers can use a free Developer Edition org. Auth must go through an External Client App, since new connected apps are restricted. Public listing of a distributed app needs AppExchange Security Review.
10 sources · audited · corrected

Salesforce is a cloud CRM and application platform (Sales Cloud and others) with a broad Platform API for accessing and automating customer data.

Auth: OAuth 2.0 authorization code (web server) flow through an External Client App (new connected apps are restricted as of Spring '26) for multi-org apps. For server-to-server use, the OAuth 2.0 JWT bearer flow (certificate-signed JWT, needs prior approval of the client app) and the client credentials flow (consumer key and secret, run as a designated integration user) are both documented for external client apps. Per-org My Domain URLs.

Conditions:

  • API access is enabled by default only in Enterprise, Performance, Unlimited and Developer editions. Professional Edition needs an API add-on.
  • Users need the API Enabled permission on their profile.
  • New connected apps can't be created as of Spring '26; use External Client Apps, or contact Support to create a connected app.
  • Publicly listing a packaged app on AppExchange requires passing the AppExchange Security Review (fee applies for paid apps). Private use in a customer's own org or an unlisted package does not require it.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: sobjects (accounts, contacts, leads, opportunities, cases), SOQL/SOSL queries, Bulk API 2.0, Apex REST, Composite, Metadata/Tooling · MCP: https://developer.salesforce.com/docs/platform/hosted-mcp-servers/guide/hosted-mcp-servers-overview.html

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: OAuth 2.0 via external client app or connected app is required for API access.
    it must be authorized as a safe visitor. To implement this authorization, use either an external client app or a connected app and an OAuth 2.0 authorization flow. source ↗
  • auth: New connected app creation is restricted as of Spring '26.
    Creating connected apps is restricted as of Spring ‘26. You can continue to use existing connected apps during and after Spring ‘26. source ↗
  • auth: JWT bearer flow supports server-to-server access with a certificate-signed JWT and prior client app approval.
    you can use the OAuth 2.0 JSON Web Token (JWT) bearer flow. This flow uses a certificate to sign the JWT request and doesn’t require explicit user interaction. source ↗
  • auth: Client credentials flow is supported for external client apps, using consumer key and secret with an integration user.
    This flow requires you to specify an integration user to run the integration. source ↗
  • access: API access is default in Enterprise, Performance, Unlimited and Developer editions; Professional needs an add-on.
    API access is enabled by default in Enterprise, Performance, Unlimited, and Developer Edition orgs. Professional Edition orgs can add API access as an add-on. source ↗
  • access: Developer Edition is free for testing and development.
    Developer Edition provides a free and easy solution so that you can use Salesforce for testing and development. source ↗
  • access: Public AppExchange listing requires passing the AppExchange Security Review.
    To list your app or provide a trial template publicly on the AppExchange, it must pass the [AppExchange Security Review] source ↗
  • api: REST API headers apply across Bulk API 2.0 and Apex REST, showing multiple API families.
    The same headers apply to Bulk API 2.0 and Apex REST web services. source ↗
  • mcp: Official hosted MCP servers from Salesforce.
    Salesforce Hosted MCP Servers give AI agents a secure, governed way to interact with Salesforce data and automation. source ↗
  • mcp: Hosted MCP servers are limited to Flex Credits customers and may be billed.
    Salesforce Hosted MCP Servers are intended only for customers with Flex Credits and you may be billed for server usage. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: suspect, The oauth2 claim is supported (external client app/connected app with OAuth 2.0). But methods lists only oauth2. Salesforce also supports the JWT bearer flow and client credentials flow, which are widely used for server-to-server integrations (service_account_or_jwt). The detail text says these 'were not verified', so the methods list is probably incomplete. Check the JWT bearer and client credentials flows.
  • access: suspect, The edition gating and the free Developer Edition are supported by the evidence. Two problems. (1) public_app_review is 'unknown'. Distributing an app across customer orgs generally goes through the AppExchange security review, so this is probably knowable and should be checked. (2) The gates list mixes in the MCP Flex Credits restriction and the 5 MB storage limit. These are not REST API access gates and could mislead. production_access=paid_plan is fair, because Professional needs an API add-on.
  • api: supported, The REST, Bulk 2.0 and Apex REST evidence matches. The broad list of types and resource families is consistent with Salesforce's well-known API surface. The rate limits are marked as not checked, which is acceptable.
  • mcp: supported, The page is Salesforce's own developer documentation for Hosted MCP Servers. It is first-party, so 'official' is justified. It shows OAuth-based connection and the Flex Credits restriction, and the note records that condition.
  • verdict: supported, build_with_conditions fits: a free Developer Edition org exists, but production API access depends on the edition (Professional needs an add-on). External Client App setup is customer admin work and is a normal step, not a gate. The blocker text is mostly fine. The record's own concerns are about auth completeness and public_app_review, not the verdict.

Changed by verification loop

  • auth.methods: oauth2 → oauth2;service_account_or_jwt
  • access.public_app_review: unknown → yes

Open questions

  • Exact current API versions and daily API request limits by edition were not checked.
  • Whether an unlisted OAuth app used across many customer orgs needs AppExchange review was not confirmed; the review is confirmed only for public AppExchange listing.

 rest, sdk, cli, webhooks

9 sources · corrected

HubSpot is a CRM platform with marketing, sales, service, CMS and operations hubs, exposing a broad REST API.

Auth: OAuth 2.0 is required for apps distributed to multiple accounts (marketplace, or private distribution capped at 10 allowlisted accounts). A static auth access token (single-account, private distribution) covers single-account installs. Developer API keys (hapikey query parameter) and client-credentials tokens are only for specific app-level features (e.g. custom channel registration, webhooks journal), not general CRM access.

Conditions:

  • OAuth is required for multi-account distribution; static auth tokens work for a single account only
  • Private-distribution OAuth apps can be installed in at most 10 accounts at a time
  • Listing on the HubSpot Marketplace is needed for broad public distribution and is subject to certification (e.g. error responses under 5% of daily requests)
  • Rate limits depend on the customer's subscription tier: 100/10s and 250k/day on Free/Starter, 190/10s on Professional/Enterprise, and 110/10s per account for marketplace OAuth apps
  • Some endpoints and features need higher HubSpot product tiers or specific hubs
  • Developer apps are built in a developer account and tested with developer test accounts

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: contacts, companies, deals, tickets, engagements, marketing, CMS, conversations, webhooks · MCP: https://mcp.hubspot.com

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: OAuth for multiple accounts; static auth access tokens for single account.
    OAuth is required for multiple accounts, while static auth access tokens are used for installing in a single account at a time. source ↗
  • auth: Private OAuth distribution capped at 10 accounts.
    If you choose this option, you can install your app in a maximum of 10 accounts at a time. source ↗
  • auth: Developer API keys (hapikey) are only for some app features and settings, not general API auth.
    Some app features and settings require a developer API key, which is available in the developer overview of your HubSpot account. source ↗
  • access: Developers can test installs with developer test accounts.
    test out an install using a developer test account. source ↗
  • access: Rate limits depend on subscription of installing account.
    The number of calls your app can make is based on your account subscription in the account it’s installed in source ↗
  • access: Marketplace OAuth apps limited per installing account.
    each HubSpot account that installs your app is limited to 110 requests every 10 seconds. source ↗
  • api: Date-versioned REST CRM API at api.hubapi.com.
    --url "https://api.hubapi.com/crm/objects/2026-03/contacts?limit=10&archived=false" source ↗
  • mcp: Vendor-hosted remote MCP server.
    The remote HubSpot MCP server is a publicly hosted endpoint for connecting external AI tools and applications to HubSpot’s CRM. source ↗
  • mcp: MCP server URL.
    Enter the HubSpot MCP server URL provided by HubSpot (`https://mcp.hubspot.com`). source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: suspect, Evidence supports OAuth2 and static auth token for single-account. But the static token from app settings is a personal/static access token (personal_token), not an api_key; 'api_key' is likely mislabeled (hapikey is deprecated/limited). Also the record's detail says hapikey exists only for a few app-level features, which isn't shown in evidence. Researcher should check whether methods should be oauth2 + personal_token.
  • access: supported, Evidence supports developer test accounts, tier-dependent rate limits, and the 110/10s marketplace limit. The 10-account private cap and marketplace review gate are consistent with cited pages. Free developer account is well known. public_app_review=yes is reasonable.
  • api: supported, Date-versioned REST API on CRM objects is shown (/crm/objects/2026-03/contacts). Broad breadth, SDKs, CLI and webhooks are consistent with known HubSpot offerings.
  • mcp: supported, HubSpot's own developer docs describe a publicly hosted remote MCP server at mcp.hubspot.com, with sign-in via the user's HubSpot account. It is first-party, so 'official' is appropriate.
  • verdict: suspect, build_now is plausible, since free developer accounts and APIs are available on all plans. However, the record's own gates (OAuth for multi-account, 10-account private cap, marketplace review for broad distribution, tier-dependent endpoints) may justify build_with_conditions for a multi-tenant toolkit. Composio would need marketplace distribution for broad use. Also check the auth methods mislabeling.

Changed by verification loop

  • auth.methods: api_key;oauth2 → oauth2;personal_token

Open questions

  • Whether a multi-tenant toolkit will need Marketplace listing for broad distribution (public_app_review=yes); per rubric this does not change the verdict.
  • Exact per-endpoint tier gating not enumerated.

Conditions:

  • access: Official FAQ: API is available on both free and paid accounts with full access and the same base limits.
    The Podio API is available to use for both paid and free Podio accounts. Both have full access to the API and the same base rate Limit A and Limit B apply. source ↗
  • access: The official pricing page still offers a $0 Free plan with 1,000 API calls/hour, and a 30-day Premium trial.
    | API Integrations | 1000 calls per hour | 1000 calls per hour Upgrade with Add-ons | 1000 calls per hour Upgrade with Add-ons | source ↗
  • access: Higher rate limits require an API Add-on.
    If you have a project that requires a higher rate limit, get an API Add-on for your related API key. source ↗
  • api: Webhooks (hooks) are supported.
    Hooks are webhooks that can call external sites when certain actions occur in Podio. source ↗
  • mcp: Vendor documents a live hosted MCP server.
    The server is live at **[https://mcp.podio.com](https://mcp.podio.com)** — just point your AI platform at it and log in with your Podio account. source ↗
  • mcp: Shared default credentials are for testing only; production should use own API key.
    This case is meant for testing, with some limits added - approximately 30-40 requests to the MCP server. For production use, we recommend connecting with your API key. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Docs confirm OAuth2 with registered client ID/secret and four flows (server-side, client-side, username/password, app auth). Token lifetimes of 8h and 28d are confirmed. Listing api_key as a method is acceptable because the client ID/secret is called an API key.
  • access: suspect, The API key page shows self-serve key generation and the rate limits are accurate. However, dev_access and production_access are both set to 'free' while the record admits the plan requirements were not verified. Podio dropped its free plan in 2024 (Citrix/Progress changes), so a free signup may no longer exist. Check whether a trial or free tier exists for new developers, and whether API access depends on the customer's plan.
  • api: supported, The docs list the areas (applications, items, tasks, files, hooks, comments, contacts, and others) and confirm webhooks (hooks) and the rate limits. The REST API is broad. The SDK claim is plausible, and the client libraries page is referenced.
  • mcp: supported, The Progress documentation page describes the Podio MCP server at mcp.podio.com/mcp. It lists 22 tools and OAuth endpoints, and states that the shared default credentials are for testing with about 30-40 requests. The vendor docs make 'official' reasonable.
  • verdict: suspect, build_now requires free or trial developer access and API on all plans. The record's own open questions say that plan dependence and free/trial availability are unverified, and I believe Podio ended its free plan, so there may be no free developer path. A more cautious verdict such as build_with_conditions or needs_research fits until it is checked. Higher rate limits also need a paid add-on, but that is not a gate for the base case.

Open questions

  • Whether OAuth apps for many customers need any Podio review is not documented; public_app_review is unknown.
  • The Free plan's 100-item cap comes only from third-party sources, not the official pricing page.

Critic (L3)

9 sources · corrected

DealCloud is Intapp's CRM and deal/relationship management platform for financial and professional services firms, with a REST API for customer-configurable data.

Auth: A user enables an API key in their DealCloud Profile, which gives a client ID and secret. These are exchanged at the per-site token endpoint using the OAuth2 client credentials flow, with scopes (data, user_management, publish, ri_import, backup). Bearer tokens last 15 minutes. There is no multi-tenant authorization-code app model. Credentials are per user and per site. The MCP server uses a separate user-delegated OAuth.

Conditions:

  • No self-serve signup or public developer program was found. Credentials come from inside a DealCloud site, so a developer needs a customer tenant or a sandbox.
  • Intapp's sandbox policy gives sandbox instances to its clients, not to outside developers. For DealCloud Financial Services a UAT instance costs extra. Third-party developers would need to go through Intapp or a customer.
  • The API user must belong to a user group with the 'API' site-area capability enabled by an admin.
  • API keys and credentials are per user and per site, so each customer must supply their own site URL, client ID and key.
  • No evidence was found on whether API access is included in every DealCloud license or sold as an add-on. Pricing is not public.
  • Rate limit of 5 requests per second on the Data, Schema and User Management endpoints.
  • The official MCP server requires a 'DealCloud with Celeste' license and the 'MCP Access' capability. It is read-only unless write tools are enabled.

Public OAuth app review: not_applicable · Resources: data (entries/records), schema (objects, fields), user management, publications, reference/data import, backup · MCP: https://api.docs.dealcloud.com/mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

Zendesk Sell uses a separate Multi-User Application registration.

Conditions:

  • Global OAuth client requests must come from a sponsored d3v- developer account, and Zendesk may restrict the client to allowed scopes.
  • Free Zendesk trial is 14 days; a non-expiring sponsored account is for Marketplace/developer partners.
  • The core Support/Help Center API is available on all plans but rate-limited by plan (Team 200/min up to Enterprise Plus 2500/min); the High Volume API add-on needs Suite Growth+/Support Professional+ and at least 10 agent seats.
  • Some APIs are tier-gated, e.g. Integration Services (ZIS) APIs on Suite Growth+ / Support Professional+; Sell has separate auth and was not checked for plan gating.
  • API tokens are deprecated in favour of OAuth.
  • The Zendesk MCP server / Zendesk Bridge is in an early access program (EAP).

Auth: Per-user personal API key from Profile settings, sent via HTTP Basic (key as username, dummy password) against the account's own domain.freshdesk.com. The public REST API docs show no OAuth2 flow, so multi-tenant use means each customer supplies their domain plus an API key.

Conditions:

  • Extra API call volume can be purchased.
  • Each customer must provide their own subdomain and an API key; there is no OAuth app model.
  • Some endpoints or features may need higher plans or enabled features; 403 is returned if a feature is not enabled.

Front is a shared inbox and customer communication platform for teams handling email, SMS, chat and other channels.

Conditions:

  • End users authorizing OAuth apps must be Front admins; only admins can create API tokens
  • SCIM auto-provisioning is not generally available and requires contacting Front
  • The Front MCP server does not support Dynamic Client Registration and needs your own OAuth client ID and secret

The Docs API key is a separate credential and is not shown in the excerpts, but it is consistent with the MCP page's mention of a Docs API key.
  • access: supported, The vendor article confirms that the Free plan has no API access, that Standard, Plus and Pro have rate limits of 200, 400 and 800 calls per minute, and that Standard lacks reporting endpoints. paid_plan is justified because the API needs a paid tier. dev_access 'free_trial' is unverified, and the record admits it, but it is flagged as an open question rather than asserted as fact.
  • api: supported, REST v2 at api.helpscout.net with JSON and HATEOAS links is confirmed, and SDKs are mentioned on the page. The resource families are admitted to be partly from general knowledge, but they are plausible and the breadth is not in dispute.
  • mcp: supported, The vendor docs describe an official Help Scout MCP, give the URL https://mcp.helpscout.net/mcp, and state that it is read-only for new connections, with per-user login, report tools on Plus/Pro, and a Docs API key for Docs tools. The 'generally available' wording is not directly shown, but the page does not contradict it.
  • verdict: supported, build_with_conditions fits: API access requires a paid tier (Standard or higher), and reporting needs Plus or Pro. The trial question is open, but the verdict is reasonable and confidence is marked medium.

Open questions

  • Is API access available during the free trial?
  • Does Help Scout require marketplace review for public OAuth apps?
  • Full resource list not verified endpoint by endpoint.

Gorgias is an ecommerce-focused customer support helpdesk with a REST API for tickets, customers and related data, plus an AI Agent and MCP server.

Auth: Private app: HTTP Basic auth with the account email as username and an API key created by an owner/admin in Settings > Account > REST API. Public app: OAuth2 authorization code flow, mandatory for public apps. Access tokens last 24 hours. The OAuth subdomain is per-account.

Conditions:

  • A 7-day free trial is available with no credit card (cap of 10 email ticket messages, no help center publishing). The trial runs on a Helpdesk plan, so API key creation should work, but this was not explicitly confirmed on a trial account.

  • OAuth2 is mandatory for public apps. The app must be approved by Gorgias before it appears in the in-product App Store.
  • Optional Sandbox/developer account needs a registered Developer Portal account and a draft app; new sandboxes are Demo accounts until Gorgias switches them to a Developer plan by email.
  • API keys are tied to an individual user's account and grant full access to the account's data.
  • Rate limits: 40 requests per 20 seconds for API keys, 80 per 20 seconds for OAuth2 apps.

21Slack
Communications and Messaging
 oauth2 free
prod: app_review
 rest, sdk, webhooks, cli
broad
 official ✓ slack Build with conditions
Developers can build and test for free. A multi-workspace product used commercially needs Slack Marketplace approval to avoid the Tier 1 limit on conversations.history and conversations.replies (1 request/minute, 15 messages), and to use the Slack MCP server.
11 sources · corrected

Slack is a team messaging and collaboration platform with a broad developer platform (Web API, Events API, bots, MCP server).

Auth: OAuth 2.0 (v2) install flow produces bot, user, and app-level tokens with granular scopes. Single-workspace apps get a token on install without any OAuth code. Multi-workspace apps need the OAuth flow. Optional token rotation. The MCP server uses confidential OAuth with the app's client_id and client_secret, issuing user tokens.

Conditions:

  • Apps for commercial distribution should be submitted and approved for listing in the Slack Marketplace; unlisted distributed apps are for pilots.
  • Since May 29, 2025, commercially distributed apps not approved for the Marketplace (new apps and new installs) get conversations.history and conversations.replies cut to Tier 1 (1 request/minute, max 15 objects per request). Marketplace apps and internal customer-built apps are not affected.
  • Slack's updated API Terms say the Marketplace is the only appropriate channel for commercial distribution of apps built with Slack APIs.
  • Workspaces can require admin approval for app installs or restrict installs to Marketplace-listed apps.
  • Slack MCP server only allows Marketplace-published or internal apps; unlisted apps are prohibited.
  • Web API rate limits are tiered per method, per workspace, per app. Special limits apply to chat.postMessage and search.
  • Enterprise Grid orgs add extra considerations.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: conversations, messages, users, files, canvases, lists, search, reactions, workflows · MCP: https://mcp.slack.com/mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: Apps must go through an authentication process to access Slack data.

  • auth: MCP server uses confidential OAuth with the app's client_id and client_secret.
    Slack supports confidential OAuth for MCP clients. You'll need to use your app's `client_id` and `client_secret` for Slack OAuth. source ↗
  • access: Undistributed single-workspace apps have full capabilities but cannot be distributed.

    apps intended for commercial distribution should be submitted and approved for listing in the Slack Marketplace. source ↗

    The new rate limits will be updated to allow 1 request per minute and will return a maximum of 15 objects per request. source ↗

    Internal customer-built applications are not impacted by these changes. source ↗

    Only apps published in the Slack Marketplace and internal apps can use MCP at this time; unlisted apps are prohibited from using MCP. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • access: suspect, Evidence shows free app creation, but production_access 'free' glosses over a material condition: commercial multi-workspace distribution is supposed to go through Marketplace review, and Slack has restricted rate limits (e.g. conversations.history, conversations.replies at ~1 req/min, limit 15) for non-Marketplace commercially distributed apps since 2025. The researcher itself did not verify this. Check the rate-limits changelog; this may warrant build_with_conditions.
  • api: supported, Broad Web API, Events API, Socket Mode, webhooks; same rate-limit tiers on all plans is quoted. Types and breadth are reasonable.
  • mcp: supported, Official Slack-hosted endpoint https://mcp.slack.com/mcp over Streamable HTTP, with Marketplace/internal-only restriction and no DCR/SSE, all quoted from docs.slack.dev.
  • verdict: suspect, build_now with high confidence conflicts with the record's own gates and open questions: Marketplace listing is expected for commercial distribution, the MCP server needs Marketplace/internal apps, and non-Marketplace apps face tightened rate limits that the researcher did not verify. A multi-tenant integrator like Composio likely needs build_with_conditions (Marketplace review/rate limits) rather than build_now. Verify and consider downgrading confidence.

24Lark (Larksuite)
Communications and Messaging
 oauth2 api_key free
prod: paid_plan
 rest, sdk, webhooks, cli
broad
 official ✓ lark Build with conditions
Custom-app API use is tier-gated. Starter-edition tenants get only 10,000 Basic API calls per month, and Advanced APIs need a purchased quota package on a Basic, Pro or Enterprise edition. Store apps are unmetered, but they need ISV qualification and app review.
11 sources · corrected

Lark is an enterprise collaboration suite (messaging, calendar, docs, video meetings, approvals, HR) with an Open Platform for building custom and store apps.

Auth: Developers create an app in the Developer Console and get an App ID and App Secret. These are exchanged for a tenant_access_token (app identity). A user_access_token comes from the standard OAuth 2.0 authorization-code flow and is used to act on behalf of a user. Custom apps serve one tenant. Store apps serve many tenants.

Conditions:

  • Since 2025 the server API is split into Unlimited, Basic and Advanced tiers. Custom (self-built) apps on the Starter edition are capped at 10,000 Basic API calls per month per tenant. Basic, Pro and Enterprise editions have no cap.
  • Advanced APIs need a separately purchased quota package, bought through Lark sales. Only Basic, Pro or Enterprise editions can buy it.
  • Authentication, Event Subscription, Contacts and AI APIs are unmetered on every edition.
  • Metering applies to enterprise custom apps only. The billing page says store apps currently have no restrictions on API calls.
  • Custom apps can only be published and used within the same enterprise.
  • Store apps, which serve all Lark tenants, are published in the Lark App Directory. Only ISV-qualified users can create them.
  • API permissions (scopes) need the tenant admin's approval. This is normal admin consent.
  • Data permissions must be configured and submitted for review before API calls succeed.
  • Some server API calls may need an IP allowlist. This was not verified.
  • Some products, such as the behavioral audit API, are limited to the Enterprise Premium plan. Other plan-specific endpoints were not enumerated.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: messaging/IM, contacts, calendar, docs/drive, approval, attendance, CoreHR, meetings/VC, bitable/sheets, AI · MCP: https://github.com/larksuite/lark-openapi-mcp

  • auth: user_access_token follows standard OAuth 2.0.
    Obtaining user_access_token follows standard OAuth 2.0. source ↗

26Discord
Communications and Messaging
 oauth2 api_key free
prod: app_review
 rest, webhooks, sdk
broad
 community ✓ discord Build with conditions
Free to build and test. To serve many customers, a public bot must be verified by Discord once it passes 100 servers, and privileged intents such as Message Content need Discord review at 10,000+ users. The bot must also be installed in each customer's guild, and some scopes are partner-only.
10 sources · corrected

Discord is a chat and community platform whose developer API lets bots and apps send messages, manage servers and receive real-time events.

Auth: OAuth2 supports the authorization code grant, implicit grant and client credentials, plus special flows for bots and webhooks. Bots install into a guild through the `bot` scope and authenticate with a bot token from the Developer Portal Bot page. User-level OAuth2 scopes are limited to identity, guilds, connections and similar.

Conditions:

  • Free developer signup: create an app in the Developer Portal, get a bot token, and install it to a test server (servers can be created for free).
  • Unverified bots can be in at most 100 servers at a time. Bots become eligible to apply for verification at 75 servers, so a public multi-tenant bot needs Discord verification to scale.
  • Privileged Intents (Guild Members, Presence, Message Content) can be self-toggled in the Developer Portal while the app reaches under 10,000 users. At 10,000+ users, Discord review and approval is required, with annual reapplication.
  • Some OAuth2 scopes (rpc, voice, dm_channels.read, identify.premium, applications.builds.upload) are only available to approved partners.
  • Reading user DMs and other user-level data is not available through normal OAuth2. Most server actions go through a bot token, which needs the bot installed in each customer's guild.
  • Heavy rate limiting applies. Apps that ignore rate limits can have their API keys revoked.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: channels, messages, guilds, members, roles, users, interactions/application commands, webhooks, voice, invites, scheduled events · MCP: https://github.com/SaseQ/discord-mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: OAuth2 grants supported, including special flows for bots and webhooks.
    We support the authorization code grant, the implicit grant, client credentials, and some modified special-for-Discord flows for Bots and Webhooks. source ↗
  • auth: Developers create an app in the Developer Portal and generate a bot token from the Bot page.
    On the [**Bot** page](https://discord.com/developers/applications/select/bot) under **Token**, click “Reset Token” to generate a new bot token. source ↗
  • access: Some OAuth scopes need Discord approval.
    Some scopes require approval from Discord to use. source ↗
  • access: Developers can create a test server for free and install their app there, so developer access is free.
    If you don’t have your own server already, you can [create one for free] source ↗
  • access: Unverified bots are capped at 100 servers; verification is required to grow beyond that.
    Without verification, a bot can be added to no more than 100 servers at a time. source ↗
  • access: Privileged intents need Discord review once an app reaches 10,000 users, with annual reapplication.
    Once your app reaches 10,000 users, you'll need to apply for Privileged Intent access. source ↗
  • api: Base REST URL.
    https://discord.com/api source ↗
  • api: Rate limit enforcement.
    API users that regularly hit and ignore rate limits will have their API keys revoked, and be blocked from the platform. source ↗
  • api: Apps use an HTTP API and a WebSocket Gateway API.
    **[HTTP API](/developers/reference#http-api)** is a REST-like API for general operations like sending and updating data in Discord, or fetching data about a resource. source ↗
  • mcp: Community MCP server that runs with the user's own bot token.
    export DISCORD_TOKEN="YOUR_DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN" source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, OAuth2 grants confirmed by the docs. The bot token from the Developer Portal is a reasonable api_key/other mapping. The record's own blocker field is a stray empty string, which is cosmetic.
  • access: suspect, The gates are mostly sourced, but bot verification and the privileged intents (Message Content, Server Members, Presence) are unconfirmed. From my knowledge, bots in 100 or more servers must be verified, and Message Content needs approval. That is a real production condition for any public multi-tenant Composio bot. Check Discord's verification docs. 'production_access: free' with public_app_review 'unknown' is weakly supported.
  • api: supported, The base URL and the rate-limit and revocation language match the cited reference page. The REST, Gateway and webhooks breadth is consistent with the public docs.
  • mcp: supported, The community status is backed by the SaseQ repo, which uses a user-supplied bot token. The 'official' MCP is correctly treated as unverified. I know of no official Discord-owned MCP server, though my knowledge may be outdated.
  • verdict: suspect, build_now is likely too optimistic. The record itself lists unconfirmed verification and privileged-intent approval at scale, partner-only scopes, and a per-guild bot install. Those fit build_with_conditions better, or at least need resolving before build_now. Also, dev access depends on an unfetched Developer Portal page, so confidence in 'free' is only medium.

Changed by verification loop

  • access.production_access: free → app_review
  • access.public_app_review: unknown → yes
  • verdict: build_now → build_with_conditions

Open questions

  • Is there a vendor-published official Discord MCP server? A third-party catalog claims one, but no Discord-owned page confirms it.
  • The verification and privileged-intent policies have changed recently (the user-count threshold and annual reapplication). Confirm current verification criteria on the App Verification help article before launch.
  • Can Composio use user-installed apps or user OAuth for a meaningful share of actions, or is a guild-installed bot required for most?

27Telegram
Communications and Messaging
 api_key other free
prod: free
 rest, sdk, webhooks
broad
 community ✓ telegram Build now
7 sources

Telegram is a cloud messaging platform with a free Bot API for building bots that send and receive messages, plus the MTProto/TDLib API for custom clients.

Auth: Bot API: each bot gets a unique token from @BotFather, and the token goes in the URL (https://api.telegram.org/bot<token>/METHOD). There is no OAuth. For user-account access over MTProto, a developer gets api_id/api_hash at my.telegram.org, and the user logs in with phone number, code and optional 2FA.

Conditions:

  • The Bot API is free. A bot token comes from @BotFather using any Telegram account.
  • The Bot API only sees chats where the bot was added or messaged. It cannot act as a user or read a user's full chat history.
  • User-account (MTProto) access needs api_id/api_hash, and each phone number can have only one. Telegram monitors unofficial clients and bans accounts for spam or flooding.
  • getUpdates and webhooks are mutually exclusive, and updates are kept for at most 24 hours.
  • Some features need extra bot permissions, such as admin rights in a chat to receive reactions or chat_member updates.

Public OAuth app review: not_applicable · Resources: messages, chats and members, updates (getUpdates/webhooks), files and media, inline queries, callback queries and buttons, polls, payments, stickers, business connections, forum topics, mini apps, bot settings · MCP: https://github.com/chigwell/telegram-mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: Each bot gets a unique authentication token when it is created, and the token is used in API requests.
    Each bot is given a unique authentication token [when it is created](https://core.telegram.org/bots/features#botfather). source ↗
  • auth: Requests are HTTPS calls to api.telegram.org with the bot token in the URL.
    All queries to the Telegram Bot API must be served over HTTPS and need to be presented in this form: `https://api.telegram.org/bot<token>/METHOD_NAME`. source ↗
  • access: MTProto API access requires an api_id and api_hash from my.telegram.org, and the API is free to use.
    We welcome all developers to use our [API](https://core.telegram.org/api#telegram-api) and source code to create Telegram-like messaging applications on our platform free of charge. source ↗
  • access: Unofficial clients and abusive use are monitored and can lead to bans.
    If you use the Telegram API for flooding, spamming, faking subscriber and view counters of channels, you **will be banned forever**. source ↗
  • api: Telegram offers the Bot API, the Telegram API and TDLib, and a Gateway API, all free except Gateway.
    The **Bot API** allows you to easily create programs that use Telegram messages for an interface. source ↗
  • api: Updates are received by long polling (getUpdates) or webhooks, which are mutually exclusive, and are stored for at most 24 hours.
    There are two mutually exclusive ways of receiving updates for your bot - the getUpdates method on one hand and webhooks on the other. source ↗
  • mcp: A community Telegram MCP server exposes 80+ tools through Telethon (MTProto), acting as a user account.
    The server currently includes 80+ MCP tools grouped into these areas: source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Bot token from @BotFather in URL is confirmed; api_key plus other (MTProto user login with api_id/api_hash) is reasonable. No OAuth is correct.
  • access: supported, Bot API and Telegram API are stated free of charge; api_id requires my.telegram.org, one per number, and abuse bans are confirmed. Gates are appropriately noted, with no paid tier or partner gate.
  • api: supported, Bot API is HTTPS/JSON with webhooks and getUpdates, mutually exclusive, 24h retention, all confirmed. Breadth broad is consistent. Rate limits are flagged as unverified.
  • mcp: supported, Community status with chigwell/telegram-mcp (Telethon, 80+ tools) is supported by the cited page. The no-official-server claim is not disproven by the evidence, and I know of no official Telegram MCP server.
  • verdict: supported, Free Bot API with instant token creation and no tier gating fits build_now. The per-user bot-token model is a design matter, not an access gate.

Open questions

  • Exact Bot API rate limits were not verified (about 30 messages per second overall and 1 message per second per chat, from general knowledge).
  • Whether a toolkit should use the per-user bot-token model or the MTProto user-session model. MTProto carries ToS and ban risk.
  • Telegram has no OAuth, so end users would have to create their own bot through @BotFather and paste the token.

Meta's WhatsApp Business Platform lets businesses send and receive WhatsApp messages, manage templates and phone numbers, and receive webhooks through the hosted Cloud API (Graph API).

Auth: Meta app (business type) with the WhatsApp use case. Docs describe OAuth-style access tokens with permissions restricting resources. A developer generates tokens for their own business (temporary tokens from the App Dashboard, or system-user tokens via Business Settings); multi-customer apps use Meta OAuth / Embedded Signup to get tokens for each customer's WhatsApp account.

Conditions:

  • A test WhatsApp account, test Messaging account and test business phone number are created automatically and need no payment method for template sends, so development is free.
  • Production sending to real users uses a real business phone number and a Messaging account with billing; a payment method is needed for charged messages. This is usage billing, not an API tier gate. Not verified: exact payment-method and phone-registration requirements for the first non-test template send.
  • Meta charges per delivered message (marketing, utility, authentication, service after a 1,000/month free tier per phone number); the pricing page states that without a payment method service messages are not delivered after the free tier is used.
  • Template messages generally require approval; non-template messages are only allowed inside the customer service window.
  • Customer-facing apps serving many businesses need Meta App Review for permissions and Tech Provider onboarding / Embedded Signup. Not verified in detail.
  • Business portfolio verification affects throughput and Official Business Account status.

Aircall is a cloud business phone and call-center platform with a public REST API, webhooks and an official read-only MCP server.

Auth: Basic Auth with an API ID and token generated by a customer in the Aircall Dashboard, for a single company's own account. OAuth (authorization code, non-expiring access token) is for multi-customer apps; client credentials are issued by Aircall only after Technology Partner approval.

Conditions:

  • Multi-customer OAuth requires approval as an Aircall Technology Partner; Aircall can decline applications at its discretion.
  • After approval and an integration plan, Aircall creates a trial developer account (4 months, extendable) and two OAuth apps (production and development); no self-serve OAuth client credentials were found.
  • Partner requirements: a finished, live product with a company website and existing customers.
  • Published apps appear in the Aircall Dashboard, and are listed in the public Marketplace after about 10 customers.
  • Basic Auth API keys work only for a customer's own account and are not meant for multi-tenant distribution; a customer can sign up for Aircall and generate one.
  • Creating API keys requires an Aircall user with that permission in the customer account.
  • Conversation intelligence endpoints and MCP tools need the Aircall AI add-on.

  • auth: Every account gets an API key and secret from the dashboard.
    When you create a Vonage account, an API key and secret will be created for you. source ↗
  • auth: Messages and Verify accept both JWT and Basic authentication, and JWT is recommended.
    Messages supports both JWT and Basic authentication, however basic authentication does not support webhooks or advanced features such as ACLs. source ↗
  • auth: JWTs are signed with the application ID and private key.
    The private key in question is generated from the application dashboard or from `vonage apps create`. source ↗
  • access: New accounts begin in trial mode with restrictions that are lifted by adding funds.
    When signing up for a new Vonage API account, your account will be in trial mode. source ↗
  • access: Trial limits sends to up to 5 verified test numbers; upgrading means adding funds.
    To remove these trial restrictions, you will need to [upgrade to a paid account] source ↗
  • api: The API reference covers many resource families, including messaging, voice, numbers, verify and account.
    Voice API Make and receive telephone calls to or from anywhere in the world. source ↗
  • api: The Number Management API is part of the reference.
    Manage all the numbers you need for your applications. Search for numbers, list and configure your purchased numbers, cancel any number you no longer need. source ↗
  • mcp: Vonage's developer site documents an MCP server for its APIs.
    The Vonage MCP Server allows AI agents and developer tools to interact with Vonage APIs through the Model Context Protocol (MCP) source ↗
  • mcp: The tooling MCP server exposes SMS, WhatsApp and RCS sending, account, number and report tools.
    This MCP server provides access to various Vonage API functionalities through the following tools: source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Evidence confirms account API key/secret from dashboard and JWT signed with application private key. api_key and service_account_or_jwt are reasonable; no OAuth model is plausible for Vonage.
  • access: supported, Support article confirms trial mode (5 verified test numbers, demo text) and removal by adding funds. Treating pay-as-you-go funds as usage cost rather than an API tier gate fits the rubric. Gates are listed.
  • api: supported, The API reference index shows broad families (Voice, Number Management, Account, Reports, Conversation, Pricing, Verify, etc.). Beta label on Messages is noted as an open question.
  • mcp: supported, The vendor developer site documents the Vonage MCP Server, and the note openly says the tooling repo is in Vonage-Community. 'Official' is backed by a vendor-hosted docs page, and the caveat is disclosed.
  • verdict: supported, Self-serve signup, free trial credit and API on all accounts support build_now. Trial limits and sender registration are normal product usage constraints, not an API-tier gate.

Open questions

  • Is there a hosted remote Vonage MCP server for the API bindings, or only the local npx/stdio one?
  • Is the Messages API still Beta, or just labelled that way in the reference index?
  • Does production sending need per-country sender registration (10DLC, alphanumeric IDs, WhatsApp Business approval) for some customers?

31Google Ads

 official ✓ googleads Build with conditions
Developer access is free (Test access on enabling the API), but production use needs Google to grant an access tier to the Cloud project. Explorer is only available if Google lets you apply, and it is capped at 2,880 ops/day with no account linking, user management or keyword planning. Real multi-tenant scale needs Standard access, which is a manual audit of about 10 business days. Upgrade rejections have been reported.
9 sources · corrected

Google Ads is Google's online advertising platform, and the Google Ads API lets applications manage campaigns, accounts and reporting programmatically.

Auth: OAuth 2.0 (multi-user authorization-code flow for apps managing other users' accounts; single-user flow) or a service account for accounts you manage. API access level comes from the Google Cloud project that owns the OAuth client or service account.

Conditions:

  • Enabling the Google Ads API in a Google Cloud project gives Test access, which works against test accounts only
  • Production accounts need Explorer access, which is not automatic: after initial sign-up, Google 'may let you apply' for it, so approval is at Google's discretion
  • Explorer access is capped at 2,880 operations per day against production accounts
  • Explorer access excludes account creation and linking, user management, keyword and reach planning, and billing services
  • Basic access (15,000 ops/day) requires OAuth brand verification for new applications
  • Standard access (unlimited ops) needs a manual audit of the tool, about 10 business days, and compliance with Required Minimum Functionality
  • Known issues after the developer-token sunset (from the previous research pass, not re-fetched): some brand-verified projects were rejected for Basic or Explorer access, and some upgrades were not honored for production calls
  • Google OAuth app verification and sensitive-scope review may apply to public multi-user apps. This was not verified in the fetched pages.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: campaigns, ad groups, ads, assets, keywords, audiences, reporting (GAQL), customers and account management, billing, keyword and reach planning · MCP: https://github.com/googleads/google-ads-mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

    Your API access levels are determined by the Google Cloud project you used to generate your OAuth credentials. source ↗

    The Standard access application involves a detailed manual audit of your application. source ↗

Explorer is capped at 2,880 ops/day and excludes account linking, user management and keyword planning. Real scale needs Standard, which is a manual audit of about 10 business days. The record also lists known issues with rejected or unhonored upgrades. This looks like build_with_conditions (access-tier application, with a Standard audit for scale), so a researcher should check it.

32Meta Ads

 official ✓ metaads Build with conditions
Development works free with a Meta developer app, but a public multi-customer production app needs Business Verification and Meta App Review for advanced access (ads_read/ads_management, and ads_mcp_management for the MCP server) and the Full access tier. Limited tier is heavily rate-limited and for development only.
11 sources

Meta Ads is Meta's advertising platform, with the Marketing API (Graph API-based) and an official ads MCP server for creating, managing and reporting on ad campaigns across Facebook, Instagram, Messenger and WhatsApp.

Auth: OAuth 2.0 via Facebook Login (ads_management / ads_read scopes) for multi-customer apps; user access tokens; system user access tokens (Business Manager) for server-to-server. Requires a Meta developer app. The ads MCP server supports OAuth (Facebook Login for Business), user tokens and Employee-role system user tokens.

Conditions:

  • Marketing API access tier: Limited access is the default on adding the product, heavily rate-limited and for development only, not for live advertisers
  • Full access (needed for production apps managing other people's ad accounts) requires App Review and usage thresholds (500 API calls in the past 15 days)
  • Advanced access to ads_read/ads_management needed to manage other people's ad accounts, approved via App Review
  • Business Verification: apps requesting advanced access or letting other Businesses grant permissions must be connected to a verified Business; until then users from other Businesses cannot grant permissions
  • Limited tier has limited Business Manager and Catalog API access and only 1 system user
  • Ads MCP server needs the ads_mcp_management permission (minimum, plus ads_read or ads_management); Advanced Access via App Review is needed to manage data on behalf of other businesses
  • Ads MCP server only supports Employee-role system user tokens; Admin-role system user tokens cannot access it

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: campaigns, ad sets, ads, ad creatives, ad accounts, insights/reporting, custom audiences, catalogs, business management, system users · MCP: https://mcp.facebook.com/ads

  • access: Agencies/partners managing other businesses' data must submit the app for review for ads_mcp_management Advanced Access.

    At a minimum, the token must be granted: source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • access: suspect, The evidence supports the tier and App Review gates. Meta usually also requires Business Verification, and often Advanced Access for other businesses' data, before ads permissions are approved. The record lists this only as an open question. The MCP claim that it was 'opened to any developer' and the 'July 2026' date are not in the shown evidence. The MCP server may also need its own app permission (ads_mcp_management) review. Check these before finalizing.
  • api: supported, The Marketing API is a Graph API REST collection covering campaigns, ad sets, creatives and related areas. The breadth claim is plausible and the cited page matches it. The claim that docs show v27.0 matches the OAuth URL in the evidence.
  • mcp: insufficient, The evidence shows the URL mcp.facebook.com/ads on developers.facebook.com docs, which supports an official Meta-hosted server. The excerpt does show OAuth, user and system-user tokens, but not the opening date or the 'any developer' claim.
  • verdict: supported, build_with_conditions fits: free dev access is automatic, and production use for other advertisers needs App Review, Advanced Access and the Full tier, which are named conditions. Business Verification is unconfirmed and may add to the conditions, but it does not change the verdict category.

Open questions

33LinkedIn Ads
Marketing, Ads, Email and Social
 oauth2 app_review
prod: app_review
 rest, webhooks
broad
 community ✓ linkedin_ads Build with conditions
Developer access requires LinkedIn to approve an Advertising API application. Managing more than 5 ad accounts needs a separate Standard tier request through a support ticket, and LinkedIn may decline it.
7 sources

LinkedIn Ads is LinkedIn's B2B advertising platform. The Marketing API lets developers manage ad accounts, campaigns and creatives, and pull ad analytics.

Auth: 3-legged OAuth 2.0 member authorization (authorization code flow). The Marketing APIs do not support 2-legged client credentials. Scopes include r_ads, rw_ads and r_ads_reporting. The member needs a suitable Ad Account role.

Conditions:

  • A developer app must apply for Advertising API access and be approved by LinkedIn. The Development tier then grants scopes.
  • Development tier: read-only for unlimited ad accounts, edit for up to 5 ad accounts, and 1 test ad account created via the API. Real ad accounts must be created in Campaign Manager.
  • Standard tier (edit unlimited ad accounts, create ad accounts) must be requested through a Developer Support Portal ticket. LinkedIn may decline even if minimum requirements are met.
  • Conversions, Matched Audiences and Lead Sync APIs each require separate access applications. Audience Insights and Media Planning are restricted to qualified developers.
  • Members need Ad Account roles (e.g. VIEWER, CAMPAIGN_MANAGER) and Company Page roles to act on data.
  • Versioned API: older versions are sunset (Marketing 202505 is sunset), so integrations must migrate regularly.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: ad accounts, campaign groups, campaigns, creatives, ad analytics/reporting, conversions, matched audiences/DMP segments, lead gen forms/lead sync, audience insights, media planning, organizations/company pages, events · MCP: https://github.com/get-mcp-ads/linkedin-ads-mcp-server

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: LinkedIn uses OAuth 2.0. Marketing APIs require member (3-legged) authorization, not 2-legged client credentials.
    Not all APIs support Application Authorization. For example, Marketing APIs you must use Member Authorization explained above. source ↗
  • access: Advertising API access is gated by applying and approval. The Development tier is limited to edits on 5 ad accounts, and the Standard tier is requested via a support ticket.
    Standard tier is designed to support campaign management solutions including edit capabilities for unlimited ad accounts. source ↗
  • access: LinkedIn may decline a Standard tier upgrade at its discretion.
    LinkedIn reserves the right to review applications and select partners at its discretion and a partner might not be upgraded even if they meet these minimum requirements. source ↗
  • access: The Development tier lets an app manage at most 5 ad accounts.
    Add up to 5 accounts to manage in the Development Tier. source ↗
  • api: The Advertising API covers ad campaigns, creatives and reporting/analytics.
    Build functionality for scalable ads management with Ad Campaigns and Creatives APIs. source ↗
  • api: The API is versioned and older versions are sunset.
    The Marketing Version 202505 (Marketing May 2025) has been sunset. source ↗
  • mcp: A community-built LinkedIn Ads MCP server exists. It is not published by LinkedIn.
    Bring your LinkedIn Ads accounts into your MCP client. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Docs confirm Marketing APIs require 3-legged member authorization, not client credentials. oauth2 is the right method.
  • access: supported, The increasing-access page supports the Development tier (read unlimited, edit 5 accounts, 1 test account) and a separate Standard tier request. It also supports LinkedIn's discretion to decline. app_review for dev and production access, with public_app_review yes, fits the rubric.
  • api: supported, The overview page confirms campaigns, creatives and reporting. The getting-started page confirms versioning and the sunset of 202505. The broader resource families and the webhooks type are plausible, but this excerpt does not show them. LinkedIn does offer lead sync and event webhooks.
  • mcp: supported, The cited GitHub repo is a third-party community server, not LinkedIn's. The record says community and notes that no vendor server was found. I know of no official LinkedIn Ads MCP server.
  • verdict: supported, build_with_conditions fits. Access needs an approved Advertising API application, and Standard tier is needed beyond 5 ad accounts and can be declined. For a multi-tenant platform this is arguably close to outreach_required or limited, but the Development tier gives real access, so the conditional verdict is defensible. The record already lists the open questions on cost and partner status.

Open questions

  • How long does Advertising API approval take, and what are the criteria for a general-purpose integration platform?
  • Can a multi-tenant platform like Composio get Standard tier, and does it need to be an approved marketing partner?
  • What are the current rate limits?
  • Is the Advertising API free of charge or tied to a minimum ad spend? This was not verified.

Private Integration Tokens (static, scoped bearer tokens) are generated in account settings for a single agency or sub-account. Legacy v1 API keys are being phased out and v1 is end-of-support.

Conditions:

  • Developers sign up for a free developer account on the Marketplace to create apps. Installing and testing needs a HighLevel agency or sub-account, which the pricing page offers as a 14-day free trial with full access to the chosen tier.
  • Official help center: API access, OAuth 2.0 and Private Integration Tokens are available on all plans (Starter $97, Unlimited $297, Founder $497, formerly Agency Pro), so the core API is not tier-gated.
  • Some endpoints are plan-restricted. Create Sub-Account and Enable SaaS for Sub-Account require the Founder (formerly Agency Pro) plan. The pricing page labels Starter and Unlimited as 'Basic API access' and the top tier as advanced.
  • Public Marketplace apps are visible and installable by all users only once approved. Private apps are for internal use.
  • Documented OAuth API v2 rate limits: 100 requests per 10 seconds and 200,000 per day.
  • Private Integration Tokens are managed in settings, and agency-admin permission to create them can be restricted per user.
  • A community report claims no API on trials. This conflicts with the pricing page, which says the trial gives full access to the chosen tier, and was not verified.

Conditions:

  • Creating API keys or OAuth apps requires an Owner, Admin, or Manager role in the Klaviyo account
  • API is available on all plans, including free, per a Klaviyo community answer (not an official doc)
  • Listing in Klaviyo's App Marketplace requires OAuth, at least 5 active installs and an app review
  • An unlisted OAuth app can be installed through its installation URL without listing
  • Some newer endpoints (Campaigns, text messaging) may need the '.pre' revision variant

Public OAuth app review: no · Resources: profiles, events, metrics, lists, segments, campaigns, flows, templates, catalogs, coupons, tags, subscriptions, reporting, images, data-privacy, reviews, forms, push-tokens · MCP: https://mcp.klaviyo.com/mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: Klaviyo offers private key auth and OAuth for server-side APIs, and a public key for client-side APIs.
    Klaviyo provides 3 methods of authentication including private key authentication and OAuth (used to call server-side APIs) and public key authentication (used to call client-side APIs). source ↗
  • auth: Private keys are sent in the Authorization header with the Klaviyo-API-Key scheme.
    Private key authentication for `/api` endpoints is performed by setting the following request header: source ↗
  • access: Managing API keys requires an Owner, Admin, or Manager role.
    To manage API keys, you must have an Owner, Admin, or Manager role on your Klaviyo account. source ↗
  • access: Only Owner, Admin, and Manager roles can create OAuth apps.
    Note that only owner, admin, and manager roles can create OAuth apps in Klaviyo. source ↗
  • access: Unlisted OAuth apps can be shared with customers via the installation URL. Marketplace listing needs OAuth and 5 active installs.
    Your app does not have to be listed to share and use your installation URL with customers. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Private key (pk_, Klaviyo-API-Key header) and OAuth2 with PKCE are confirmed. Public key is client-only and correctly excluded from methods.
  • access: supported, Role requirement, unlisted-app installation URL, and the 5-install marketplace requirement are all confirmed. API access on the free plan rests on a community answer from a Klaviyo staff-style reply. That is weak but consistent with my knowledge. The record flags it as unofficial.
  • api: supported, Rate limits, scopes and the JSON:API structure are confirmed. The broad resource list is plausible. The record already says webhooks were not verified. Klaviyo webhooks exist as a flow action, not as a general API subscription. The 'webhooks' type may be loosely stated.
  • mcp: supported, Vendor docs confirm the official remote MCP at mcp.klaviyo.com/mcp, with OAuth DCR, Streamable HTTP and the role restriction. The tool count is unverified but minor.
  • verdict: supported, Self-serve keys and OAuth, with no sales or partner gate, and the API is available on all plans, including free. build_now fits. The marketplace review is correctly kept out of the verdict.

Open questions

  • Webhooks were not verified in the fetched pages.
  • Whether Klaviyo OAuth apps have install caps before marketplace listing was not fully checked. The docs say unlisted apps can be shared via installation URL.

Pinterest is a visual discovery and social platform where users save Pins to boards, and businesses run ads and shopping catalogs, with a broad REST API v5.

Auth: OAuth 2.0 authorization code grant (also client credentials) with app ID and secret, scopes such as boards:read and pins:read. Access tokens need refreshing. Trial apps can also generate 24-hour test tokens.

Conditions:

  • Trial access: Pins and boards created are visible only to the creator and rate limits are daily/lower; Standard access upgrade is needed to offer Pinterest features to users
  • Apps registered on or after Sept 14, 2026 may get 403 PINNER_DATA_ACCESS_DENIED when reading boards/Pins of non-business users without Pinterest authorization
  • Test tokens expire after 24 hours; sandbox available for some endpoints

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: pins, boards, board sections, user account, ad accounts, campaigns, ad groups, ads, audiences, customer lists, catalogs and feeds, product groups, analytics and reporting, conversion events, trending keywords, media · MCP: https://developer.pinterest.com/about-pinterest-mcp

  • access: Developers must request Trial access and be approved before they can manage their app and get credentials.

  • access: supported, Trial access needs request-form approval by Pinterest, and Standard needs an upgrade. This matches the cited pages. app_review is appropriate, and public_app_review yes is consistent.
  • api: supported, The v5 reference page lists pins, boards, ads, campaigns, audiences, catalogs, analytics and conversions. This supports a broad REST API.

The access-tier review is a vendor review of a public app and should not by itself make this outreach_required. One thing to check: the gate about apps registered on or after Sept 14, 2026 is not in the excerpts shown. Today is 2026-11-23, so that date is past, and the researcher should confirm the wording against the source.

Threads is Meta's microblogging social network; its API lets apps publish posts, read posts and profiles, manage replies, search, and fetch insights on behalf of authorizing users.

Auth: OAuth 2.0 authorization code flow via the Threads Authorization Window gives short-lived Threads user access tokens, exchangeable for refreshable long-lived tokens. Uses the Threads app ID and secret from a Meta app. Scopes include threads_basic, threads_content_publish, threads_manage_replies, threads_read_replies and threads_manage_insights.

Conditions:

  • Developer creates a Meta app with the Access the Threads API use case. Threads Testers (users with a role on the app) can grant permissions without App Review.
  • For app users without a role on the app, each permission must be approved via Meta App Review and the app must be published.
  • Tech Providers whose apps serve clients or other business portfolios must complete App Review requirements before publishing.
  • Permission grants last 90 days for public profiles. Long-lived tokens must be refreshed, and private profiles must re-grant.
  • Rate limits: 250 API-published posts per rolling 24h per profile; call counts scale with impressions.
  • Webhooks require a published app but not App Review.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: posts and publishing, replies and reply management, reposts and quote posts, profiles, keyword search, mentions, insights, locations, oEmbed, webhooks · MCP: https://github.com/baguskto/threads-mcp
