  • access: Threads testers can grant permissions freely. Other app users require App Review approval and a published app.
    For app users without a role on your app to grant your app these permissions, each permission must first be approved through the source ↗
  • access: Developers add Threads Testers through the app dashboard to test without review.
    To test your app with a Threads user, you must first send an invitation to the Threads user’s profile and accept the invitation. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Docs say Threads user access tokens conform to OAuth 2.0 via the Authorization Window; the detail on short-lived and long-lived tokens matches the page.
  • access: supported, The docs confirm testers can grant permissions without review. Other users need App Review per permission and a published app. Tech Provider steps are shown. Dev access is free, since a Meta app costs nothing. Business verification is flagged as an open question, which is appropriate.
  • api: supported, The host, the 250 posts per 24h cap and the impressions-based call formula (minimum 10) all match the overview page. The permission list supports the broad resource families. The webhooks claim is not shown in the excerpts, but it is plausible and consistent with the known Threads API.
  • mcp: supported, The baguskto/threads-mcp README describes a third-party server wrapping the official API. Community status is right, and no Meta-published server is evidenced.
  • verdict: supported, Free dev access with App Review required for non-tester users is a named condition. That fits build_with_conditions, since App Review is vendor review of a public OAuth app. Under the rubric, public_app_review should not change the verdict, which makes build_now arguably the stricter fit. Still, Testers-only access without review is a material production gate, so build_with_conditions is defensible.

Open questions

  • Whether business verification is required for Tech Provider status. The create-an-app page links to it but does not state it.
  • Whether keyword search, mentions and profile discovery permissions have extra App Review requirements beyond the standard process.
  • Exact per-app call-count limits. The docs give only the impressions-based formula.

Twilio SendGrid is a cloud email delivery platform with an Email API/SMTP relay for transactional email and Marketing Campaigns for contacts, campaigns and automation.

  • Customers create their own scoped API key.

41Shopify

9 sources · corrected

Shopify is a commerce platform for online and retail stores, with APIs to manage products, orders, customers and storefronts.

Auth: GraphQL Admin API: OAuth (usually token exchange for embedded apps, authorization code grant for non-embedded) gives a per-shop access token sent in X-Shopify-Access-Token. Storefront API: public/private tokens or tokenless. Customer Account API: buyer OAuth 2.0 + PKCE. Partner API: client created in organization settings. Legacy admin-created custom apps can no longer be created for new apps.

Conditions:

  • Public distribution requires Shopify App Store review/approval and registration with a one-time fee
  • Custom distribution (single store or stores in one Plus organization) needs no approval but cannot use Shopify's app billing
  • Merchants must install the app via OAuth and grant access scopes; new apps cannot rely on store-created custom app tokens
  • Protected customer data (customers, orders, etc.): public apps require Shopify review for level 1 and level 2 (name, address, phone, email fields); custom apps have it available
  • Protected customer data access in development needs no review for apps installed only on dev stores
  • Dev stores are test-only, cannot process real transactions or be converted to production
  • GraphQL Admin API is rate limited by calculated query cost

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: products, orders, customers, fulfillment, inventory, discounts, metafields, storefront cart/checkout · MCP: https://shopify.dev/docs/agents/shop-mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: Admin API auth is OAuth, usually token exchange.

  • access: Public distribution requires App Store registration with a fee.
    Public distribution also requires registering for the Shopify App Store, which has a one-time fee. source ↗
  • access: Dev stores can't become production stores.
    Dev stores can't be converted to production stores. source ↗
  • access: Protected customer data needs no review for dev-store-only apps.
    You don't need to submit a request for review for apps that are installed only on development stores. source ↗
  • access: Level 1 protected customer data requires review for public apps and is always available for custom apps.
    | 1 | Requires review | Always available | Always available | source ↗
  • api: GraphQL Admin API is rate-limited by query cost.
    The GraphQL Admin API is rate-limited using calculated query costs, measured in cost points. source ↗
  • mcp: Dev MCP runs locally with no auth.
    The server runs locally and doesn't require authentication. source ↗
  • mcp: Storefront MCP was replaced by a per-store UCP MCP endpoint.
    UCP now provides these capabilities at `https://{shop}/api/ucp/mcp`. source ↗
  • mcp: Shop MCP is Shopify's hosted MCP server and needs no API key.
    You don't need an API key or a custom UCP profile. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • access: supported, Partner/dev account and dev stores are free. Public app review and the one-time registration fee are recorded as public_app_review, not as an API gate. Dev-store limits are accurately quoted.

  • mcp: suspect, Dev MCP is official, and the evidence supports a local, no-auth server. The record does not cover Shopify's other official MCP servers: the Storefront MCP, which is hosted per store at /api/mcp and is used for agentic commerce, and the Catalog/Checkout MCP. Check whether any of these should be recorded; the record's own open question flags this. The 'official' status is fine, but the note that no store-operations MCP exists may be wrong.
  • verdict: suspect, build_now is plausible given free dev stores and an API on all plans. However, protected customer data (orders and customers) needs approval or declarations. Also, new apps can no longer use store-created custom app tokens, so each merchant must install via an app and OAuth. Public distribution needs review. These are conditions the record itself flags as unverified. Consider build_with_conditions, or confirm that protected customer data access is not a material barrier.

42WooCommerce
Ecommerce
 api_key free
prod: free
 rest, webhooks, sdk
broad
 official – Build now
9 sources

WooCommerce is the open-source WordPress ecommerce plugin for running online stores, with products, orders, customers and coupons managed through its REST API.

Auth: A store admin generates a consumer key and secret in WooCommerce > Settings > Advanced > REST API. These are sent with Basic Auth over HTTPS, or with OAuth 1.0a one-legged signing over HTTP. Apps can also redirect a merchant to /wc-auth/v1/authorize, which POSTs the generated keys back to the app's callback URL. WordPress Application Passwords are used for the MCP endpoint.

Conditions:

  • Self-hosted: each merchant's store has its own URL and credentials; there is no central vendor-hosted API or app marketplace.
  • Requires WooCommerce 3.5+, WordPress 4.4+ and pretty permalinks (default permalinks will not work).
  • HTTPS is recommended. Over plain HTTP, OAuth 1.0a one-legged signing is required.
  • The wc-auth endpoint lets an app request keys without manual copying. The merchant approves it and the keys are POSTed to the app's HTTPS callback_url. No vendor review is mentioned.
  • The MCP integration is in developer preview and needs the separate MCP Adapter plugin plus a WordPress Application Password.

Public OAuth app review: no · Resources: orders, products, product variations, product categories/tags/attributes, customers, coupons, refunds, reports, tax rates/classes, webhooks, settings, payment gateways, shipping zones/methods, system status · MCP: https://developer.woocommerce.com/docs/features/mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • identity: WooCommerce's REST API is integrated with the WordPress REST API. The current version is v3.
    WooCommerce (WC) 2.6+ is fully integrated with the WordPress [REST](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representational_State_Transfer) API. source ↗
  • auth: A store admin generates a consumer key and secret in WooCommerce > Settings > Advanced > REST API, with read/write permissions. No paid tier is involved.
    Go to the **REST API** tab and click **Add key** source ↗
  • auth: Over HTTPS, the consumer key and secret are sent with HTTP Basic Auth.
    You may use HTTP Basic Auth by providing the REST API Consumer Key as the username and the REST API Consumer Secret as the password. source ↗
  • auth: An application authentication endpoint lets any app have a user grant access and receive generated API keys by callback.
    This endpoint can be used by any APP to allow users to generate API keys for your APP. source ↗
  • access: The REST API ships with WooCommerce itself. The requirements are a recent WooCommerce and WordPress and pretty permalinks. The API is not tied to a plan or a separate purchase.
    Pretty permalinks in **Settings > Permalinks** (default permalinks will not work) source ↗
  • api: The REST API v3 covers many resource families, including coupons, customers, orders, products and variations, categories, tags, reviews, reports, refunds, tax, webhooks, settings, payment gateways, shipping and system status. Official JS, PHP, Python and Ruby libraries exist.
    Product attribute terms source ↗
  • api: Responses are paginated, with 10 items per page by default.
    Requests that return multiple items will be paginated to 10 items by default. source ↗
  • mcp: WooCommerce's developer docs describe an MCP integration, in developer preview, that works through the WordPress MCP Adapter plugin. Remote use authenticates with a WordPress Application Password.
    With the MCP Adapter plugin, AI clients can discover and run these store operations through the Model Context Protocol (MCP), subject to WordPress and WooCommerce permissions. source ↗
  • mcp: The MCP feature is flagged as developer preview and its details may change.
    The MCP implementation in WooCommerce is currently in developer preview. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Docs confirm consumer key/secret generated by admin, Basic Auth over HTTPS, OAuth 1.0a over HTTP, and the wc-auth endpoint. api_key is a reasonable method classification; Application Password mention for MCP is accurate.
  • access: supported, REST API ships with the free open-source plugin; requirements are only version and permalinks. No paid tier or vendor review is evidenced. The self-hosted nature is noted correctly as a gate.
  • api: supported, Resource list, v3 path, pagination default of 10, and official libraries match the cited docs index. Webhooks are a resource in the docs.
  • mcp: supported, Official WooCommerce developer docs describe the MCP integration, flagged as developer preview, requiring the separate MCP Adapter plugin plus an Application Password. The record states these caveats accurately.
  • verdict: supported, Free, self-hosted REST API available on every store with self-serve key generation and no vendor gate. build_now fits the rubric. The open questions on managed hosts are minor and do not change it.

Open questions

  • Is the MCP integration (developer preview) stable enough to rely on? The vendor docs say details may change.
  • Do managed hosts such as WordPress.com or other hosts block REST API keys or the wc-auth endpoint on some plans? Not checked.
  • The woocommerce.com/document/woocommerce-rest-api page was not fetched directly. The developer.woocommerce.com mirror was used instead.

43BigCommerce
Ecommerce
 oauth2 api_key free_trial
prod: free
 rest, graphql, webhooks, sdk

 official ✓ bigcommerce Build now
7 sources

BigCommerce is a SaaS ecommerce platform with REST and GraphQL APIs for catalog, orders, customers, carts, checkout, promotions and storefront management.

Auth: Store-level API accounts: the merchant creates client_id/client_secret and a static access_token in the control panel for one store. App-level OAuth2 authorization-code flow (Developer Portal app, token from login.bigcommerce.com/oauth2/token) for multi-store apps. Account-level API accounts also exist.

Conditions:

  • Developer access: sign up for a free BigCommerce trial store (partners can get sandbox stores) and create API credentials under Settings > API Accounts.
  • Store-level API accounts are normally created only by store owners and authorized users.
  • Apps approved for public distribution can generate access_tokens on behalf of other stores. Unapproved apps only install in stores registered to the app owner's email, and must be submitted for approval to reach other stores.
  • Merchants must approve all OAuth scopes when installing an app; they cannot pick and choose.
  • Some APIs need a specific account type, e.g. the GraphQL Account API needs an account-level token.
  • Store Management MCP is beta and must be enabled by the store owner.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: catalog/products, orders, customers, carts, checkouts, channels, inventory, promotions, price lists, shipping, currencies, redirects, scripts, settings, abandoned carts · MCP: https://docs.bigcommerce.com/developer/docs/beta/store-management-mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: BigCommerce offers store-level, app-level and account-level OAuth-based API accounts.
    BigCommerce offers three types of OAuth-based API accounts to developers who wish to use BigCommerce's REST APIs: store-level credentials, app-level credentials, and account-level credentials. source ↗
  • auth: Multi-store apps use a code grant flow to get a per-store access token.
    To generate an access_token for the merchant’s store, send a `POST` request to `https://login.bigcommerce.com/oauth2/token`. source ↗
  • access: Developers can sign up for a free trial store and create credentials in the control panel.
    Sign up for a free BigCommerce trial store to test API calls and build integrations without affecting live data. source ↗
  • access: Public availability of an app is gated by approval, which lets it create tokens for other stores.
    when an app is approved to be publicly available for additional stores to install, it can generate `access_token`s *on behalf* of store owners and authorized users. source ↗
  • api: The REST Management API spans many resource families, including carts, channels, checkouts, customers, inventory, orders, price lists, promotions and shipping.
    Use `/abandoned-carts/{token}` on headless storefronts to retrieve the `cart_id` using the abandoned cart `token` passed to the storefront when a shopper clicks an abandoned cart email link. source ↗
  • api: The API supports both REST and GraphQL, depending on the account type.
    | [GraphQL Admin API](#access-tokens) | × | × | | source ↗
  • mcp: BigCommerce publishes an official Store Management MCP in beta, which the store owner must enable.
    Only the store owner can turn on Store Management MCP — this step can’t be delegated to another user. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Docs confirm OAuth-based API accounts (store/app/account level) and authorization-code flow at login.bigcommerce.com/oauth2/token. Store-level static client_id/secret/access_token is reasonably listed as api_key; oauth2 for app flow.
  • access: supported, Docs say to sign up for a free trial store and create credentials under Settings > API Accounts. Store-owner constraint and approval for public apps are quoted. Gates are reasonable; trial duration is already listed as an open question.
  • api: supported, Evidence shows REST Management and GraphQL Admin/Storefront APIs across account types, and the resource families listed are plausible for BigCommerce. The cited abandoned-carts page only shows one family, but the breadth is well known.
  • mcp: supported, The vendor docs page describes an official Store Management MCP in beta, enabled by the store owner under Settings > API and MCP. The 'official' status is backed by a first-party source.
  • verdict: supported, Free trial store gives API access and API is not tied to a paid tier, so build_now fits. The store-owner enablement for MCP only affects the optional MCP. The only mild doubt is the unverified trial-period API access, already noted as an open question.

Open questions

  • Rate limits and plan-specific API limits were not verified.
  • Whether a trial store has full API access for the whole trial period was not checked.
  • The exact approval process and timeline for App Marketplace listing were not reviewed.

44Salesforce Commerce Cloud
Ecommerce
 oauth2 api_key partner_or_sales
prod: free
 rest, sdk, cli
broad
 official – Outreach required
No self-serve or free developer route to a B2C Commerce instance. Dev credentials and sandboxes require a licensed tenant and purchased sandbox credits through a Salesforce Account Executive. Each customer's Account Manager admin must then create an API client or SLAS client. No central OAuth app exists.
10 sources · corrected

Salesforce B2C Commerce is an enterprise ecommerce platform whose SCAPI REST APIs cover shopper storefront and admin/merchant operations.

Auth: Two token paths. Shopper APIs use SLAS, a Shopper Login and API Access Service. A SLAS public or private client is created in the SLAS Admin UI or API, and a private client has a secret. Admin APIs use an Account Manager API client with client ID and secret and allowed scopes, via OAuth2 client credentials. The client needs the Salesforce Commerce API role for specific instances. Each client is per tenant, with its own realm, instance, short code and org ID. OCAPI is a legacy alternative and needs its own separate API client.

Conditions:

  • No self-serve developer signup or free developer sandbox was found. A developer needs a B2C Commerce instance, obtained via a licensed tenant. On-demand sandboxes need purchased sandbox credits via the Account Executive, and the realm ID comes from the AE or CSM.
  • The docs offer a shared public demo sandbox, but only for read-only Shopper API trials. It does not provide developer credentials.
  • Trailhead notes that B2C Commerce is not available in the Trailhead Playground; customer or partner developers are directed to ask their manager for sandbox access. No documented self-serve partner sandbox was found.
  • An Account Manager Account Administrator must create the API client and assign the Salesforce Commerce API role and organization/instance filters. The admin must also grant the SLAS Organization Administrator role to create SLAS clients.
  • The API is included for B2C Commerce customers at no extra cost, but each customer gets credentials only from their own tenant. There is no single multi-tenant public app.
  • Shopper and admin scopes must not be mixed on the same client. SCAPI clients and OCAPI clients must be separate. Account Manager client secrets must be alphanumeric.
  • Tokens are issued per instance, with one instance per token request.

Public OAuth app review: no · Resources: shopper (baskets, products, customers, search, orders), inventory availability, orders, admin APIs (14+ families) · MCP: https://github.com/SalesforceCommerceCloud/b2c-developer-tooling/tree/main/packages/b2c-dx-mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: Shopper APIs use SLAS; Admin APIs use Account Manager.
    Shopper APIs are secured with the Shopper Login and API Access Service (SLAS), but SCAPI Admin APIs are secured with Account Manager (AM) instead. source ↗
  • auth: Admin API use requires an administrator to create an API client.
    To use the SCAPI Admin APIs, you (or someone with administrator access) must first create an API client by following these steps: source ↗
  • auth: Private SLAS clients get an auto-generated secret.
    Choosing **BFF or Web App** creates a private client (secret generated automatically). source ↗
  • access: API is included at no extra cost for B2C Commerce customers.
    For B2C Commerce customers, the API is available at no extra cost. source ↗
  • access: A shared demo sandbox is available for trying the API if you lack an instance.
    If you don’t have a B2C Commerce instance, try the demo sandbox used in the example above. source ↗
  • access: On-demand sandboxes require purchased credits arranged via an Account Executive.
    To use on-demand sandboxes, you must purchase sandbox credits. Contact your Account Executive for more information. source ↗
  • access: Realm ID needed to create sandboxes comes from the AE or CSM, i.e. sales-led access.
    Each realm has a unique four-character ID that you can get from your Account Executive (AE) or Customer Success Manager (CSM). source ↗
  • api: SCAPI is a set of RESTful APIs for storefronts, merchant tools and integrations.
    The B2C Commerce API (SCAPI) is a set of RESTful APIs for building storefronts, merchant tools, and integrations on the B2C Commerce platform. source ↗
  • api: Admin API families include Inventory Availability and Orders.
    Inventory Availability](https://developer.salesforce.com/docs/commerce/commerce-api/references?meta=inventory-availability:Summary), [Orders](https://developer.salesforce.com/docs/commerce/commerce-api/references?meta=orders:Summary) source ↗
  • mcp: Official B2C DX MCP server exists in the @salesforce/b2c-dx-mcp package.
    The B2C DX MCP Server is implemented in the `@salesforce/b2c-dx-mcp` package and provides these tools. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Evidence supports SLAS (shopper, public/private clients) and Account Manager client credentials for Admin APIs. oauth2 is right. The api_key label is loose, since SLAS/AM client ID and secret are OAuth2 clients, but it is harmless.
  • access: suspect, dev_access=paid_plan is plausibly right, since on-demand sandboxes need purchased credits and the demo sandbox is read-only shopper only. But production_access=free is awkward: the API is included at no extra cost for existing customers, yet the platform needs a B2C Commerce licence. Under the rubric a paid product is not an API gate, so the main gate is no self-serve dev access, not paid_plan. Check whether a partner or trial sandbox route exists (Salesforce Partner Program, Trailhead, or a free developer sandbox) and how dev_access should be labelled. Evidence on this was not found.
  • api: supported, The quoted evidence supports REST SCAPI and the listed Admin families (Assignments, Catalogs, Customers, Inventory Availability, Orders, Products, plus 8 more). The SDK/CLI claims are plausible. The record admits rate limits and webhooks were not checked.
  • mcp: supported, Salesforce's own docs page describes the B2C DX MCP server, implemented in the @salesforce/b2c-dx-mcp package, so official is supported. The record correctly notes it is developer-workflow focused.
  • verdict: supported, build_with_conditions fits: the API is free for licensed customers, but credentials need a licensed tenant or paid sandbox credits plus admin-created clients. This is closer to outreach_required if there is truly no non-customer dev access. Because the demo sandbox gives some read-only shopper access and a licensed customer can self-serve, build_with_conditions is defensible. The access dev_access label should be rechecked, but the verdict holds.

  • access.dev_access: paid_plan → partner_or_sales
  • verdict: build_with_conditions → outreach_required

Open questions

  • Is there a Salesforce partner program sandbox or realm for ISVs that is free or included? Not found in the docs.
  • Trailhead claims come from search snippets, not fetched pages.
  • Rate limits, quotas and webhook support were not verified.
  • Whether existing B2C Commerce customers' own tenants make this effectively build_with_conditions for a bring-your-own-credentials toolkit.

45Magento (Adobe Commerce)
Ecommerce
 oauth2 api_key personal_token service_account_or_jwt free
prod: admin_approval
 rest, graphql, soap, webhooks, sdk
broad
 community – Build with conditions
Credentials are per merchant. The merchant admin must create an Integration and either use OAuth 1.0a signing or enable the standalone-bearer setting. SaaS needs Adobe IMS/Developer Console setup. Developer access without a licensed Adobe Commerce instance is only confirmed for Magento Open Source.
8 sources · corrected

Adobe Commerce (formerly Magento) is an ecommerce platform exposing REST, GraphQL and SOAP APIs for catalog, orders, customers and more.

Auth: PaaS/on-prem/Open Source: a merchant admin creates an Integration, which issues consumer key/secret and access token/secret for OAuth 1.0a signing. Standalone bearer use of the integration token is disabled by default and must be enabled by the merchant via a config setting. Admin and customer tokens come from username/password token endpoints. SaaS (Cloud Service) requires Adobe IMS access tokens, either user-based or server-to-server.

Conditions:

  • Free developer access is only confirmed for self-hosted Magento Open Source. Adobe Commerce (PaaS/Cloud/SaaS) is a licensed product, and no free sandbox for it was verified.
  • Credentials are per merchant store: the merchant admin must create an Integration and grant ACL resources.
  • Integration access tokens are not usable as standalone bearer tokens unless the merchant enables the oauth/consumer/enable_integration_as_bearer setting. Otherwise requests must be OAuth 1.0a-signed.
  • Admin tokens expire after 4 hours by default and customer tokens after 1 hour.
  • SaaS (Cloud Service) requires Adobe IMS authentication (Adobe Developer Console setup) and has a smaller REST endpoint set. Customer and guest REST APIs are replaced by GraphQL.

Public OAuth app review: no · Resources: products/catalog, orders, customers, carts/quotes, inventory, CMS pages/blocks, categories, invoices/shipments · MCP: https://github.com/magenable/magento-adobe-commerce-mcp-server

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: Merchant-created integrations generate four OAuth credentials.
    When a merchant creates and activates an integration, Commerce generates a consumer key, consumer secret, access token, and access token secret. source ↗
  • auth: Third-party applications use OAuth 1.0a.
    Third-party applications authenticate with [OAuth 1.0a](https://developer.adobe.com/commerce/webapi/get-started/authentication/gs-authentication-oauth). source ↗
  • access: Standalone bearer use of integration tokens is disabled by default and requires a merchant config change.
    This behavior has been disabled by default due to the security implications of a never-expiring access token. source ↗
  • access: The bearer setting can be enabled by the merchant via CLI.
    bin/magento config:set oauth/consumer/enable_integration_as_bearer 1 source ↗
  • access: SaaS requires Adobe IMS for authentication.
    In Adobe Commerce as a Cloud Service, you must use Adobe Identity Management Service (IMS) to source ↗
  • api: REST API covers PaaS and SaaS editions, with a smaller endpoint set in SaaS.
    Primarily the Cloud Service REST API differs in that it includes a smaller subset of endpoints. source ↗
  • identity: REST URL structure for PaaS/on-prem is per-host.
    `https://<host>/rest/<store-view-code>/<endpoint>` source ↗
  • mcp: Community MCP server for Magento/Adobe Commerce REST API.
    This is a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server that connects to a Magento Open Source or Adobe Commerce REST API source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Evidence supports OAuth 1.0a integrations with four credentials, admin/customer tokens via login credentials, standalone bearer disabled by default, and IMS for SaaS. Labeling OAuth 1.0a as 'oauth2' is imprecise (it's 1.0a, nearest bucket 'other'), but the detail text is accurate. Minor: 'api_key' and 'personal_token' are loose mappings.
  • access: suspect, 'free' for production_access is questionable: Adobe Commerce (PaaS/Cloud/SaaS) is a licensed product and there is no free dev sandbox evidence for SaaS; the record itself lists that as an open question. Free access holds only via self-hosted Magento Open Source. Also the integration-token-as-bearer needs merchant config enablement (an unusual admin enablement), and SaaS needs IMS/Adobe Developer Console setup. Please verify that dev access applies for the Adobe Commerce edition, not just Open Source.
  • api: supported, REST across PaaS/on-prem/Open Source, smaller SaaS subset plus GraphQL, and host-based URL are all shown in the cited pages. SOAP, webhooks and SDK types are not in the excerpts but are consistent with known Adobe Commerce capabilities.
  • mcp: supported, Community server evidence is shown (magenable repo), and the note honestly says only that repo was fetched. The Adobe developer-tooling MCPs are correctly distinguished from store-data servers. From my knowledge, no official Adobe store-data MCP is known, but worth a quick check given Adobe's active MCP work.
  • verdict: suspect, build_now seems too optimistic. Each merchant has its own host, admin must create Integration, bearer use is disabled by default (requires OAuth 1.0a signing or admin enabling a setting), and SaaS uses IMS with unclear sandbox access. Open questions admit access for Adobe Commerce (non-Open Source) is unverified. build_with_conditions (merchant admin enablement / per-store setup, edition differences) fits the rubric better, as 'unusual admin enablement' is a named condition.

Changed by verification loop

  • access.production_access: free → admin_approval
  • verdict: build_now → build_with_conditions

Open questions

  • Is there a free sandbox or trial for licensed Adobe Commerce (PaaS or SaaS) for developers?
  • How does a third-party developer get Adobe Developer Console / IMS server-to-server credentials for a customer's SaaS tenant?
  • What are the API rate limits?

Squarespace is a website builder with commerce, domains and reseller features, exposing REST Commerce APIs, webhooks and a hosted MCP server.

Auth: API keys are generated by a merchant in Settings > Advanced > Developer API Keys for custom applications on their own site. Webhook Subscriptions API is OAuth only.

  • Whether OAuth client registration/testing itself needs a paid Squarespace plan is not stated in the docs

47Ecwid

 oauth2 api_key free

Customers' stores need a paid Ecwid plan for API access; sources suggest Venture or higher, but this is unconfirmed. Developers can get a free upgrade by emailing Ecwid. Reaching many customers through OAuth means passing App Market verification for a public app.
10 sources · corrected

Ecwid is an ecommerce platform that adds online stores to existing websites and social channels, and has a REST API for store data.

Auth: OAuth 2.0 for public apps that serve multiple stores. For a single store, a custom app gives a non-expiring secret token (and a public token) copied from the app dashboard and sent as a Bearer token. Calls also need the store ID.

Conditions:

  • Only stores on paid plans can access the API; free-plan stores cannot
  • Third-party guides and a search snippet of the Ecwid developer site say API access needs Venture or higher (Starter is not listed). I could not fetch the official plan-availability page to confirm this, so the tier threshold is unverified
  • Ecwid gives developers a free upgrade to a paid plan on request by email, for store customizations, integrations or public App Market apps
  • There is no test mode, so a separate test store is recommended
  • Custom apps are private to one store and cannot be installed in other stores or published
  • Serving many stores needs a public app published in the Ecwid App Market, which requires technical verification by the App Market team

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: products, categories, orders, customers, discount coupons, store profile and settings, shipping and payment methods, carts/abandoned carts, inventory · MCP: https://ecwid.usefulapi.io/mcp

    There is no **test mode** in Ecwid API, so we recommend setting up a separate test store. source ↗
  • api: Rate limit of 600 requests per minute per token.
    We accept up to **600 requests per minute** **per token**. source ↗
  • api: The REST API covers store data, payment and shipping methods, and storefront settings.
    Ecwid REST API allows you to access and manage any store data, payment and shipping methods, and storefront settings. source ↗

    Read store products, orders, customers, categories and coupons; adjust inventory and update orders. Hosted by **usefulapi** source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Docs confirm OAuth 2.0 for public apps and a custom-app secret/public token for single stores. api_key is a reasonable label for the custom-app token (could also be personal_token), but the evidence supports the flow.
  • access: suspect, The evidence supports 'only paid stores can access API' and the free developer upgrade. But the rubric says a paid product is not an API gate unless the API needs a specific higher tier or add-on. Here the evidence shows only that a paid plan is required, not which tier. The record itself admits it did not check per-tier access. I recall that Ecwid's API is available on all paid plans (Venture and up), and that the free plan lacked API. Check whether paid_plan is the right label for a plain 'any paid plan' requirement. Also check whether the free dev upgrade makes dev access effectively free-on-request, so it would be better described as a request or trial. The 'yes' for public_app_review is plausible, since the App Market is reviewed, but it has no quoted evidence.
  • api: supported, The REST API overview supports the broad scope (store data, payment/shipping methods, storefront settings), the 600 requests/min/token limit, 429 with Retry-After, and gzip. The shown excerpts do not cover webhooks, the SDK type or some resource families such as carts/checkout, but Ecwid does have webhooks and these are plausible.
  • mcp: supported, The usefulapi page supports a third-party hosted MCP server using the store ID and secret token, so 'community' fits. I'm not aware of an official Ecwid or Lightspeed MCP server. The other servers (PopMCP, Vinkius, Zapier) and the free tier are not shown in the excerpt.
  • verdict: suspect, build_with_conditions rests on 'a paid plan is required', which the rubric says is not an API gate unless a specific higher tier is needed. Ecwid offers free paid-plan upgrades for developers, and the record found no evidence of a higher-tier requirement. The result could therefore be build_now, since dev access is free on request and the API is on all paid plans. Alternatively it could stay build_with_conditions because the free upgrade is by email request and customers need paid plans. The record leaves the per-tier question open, so the verdict can't be confirmed until the researcher checks it.

Changed by verification loop

  • access.dev_access: paid_plan → free

Open questions

Auth: Developers register an application in Settings > Advanced (name, icon, Redirect URI) and get a client ID and secret. 49Amazon Selling Partner
Ecommerce
 oauth2 free
prod: free
 rest, sdk, webhooks
broad
 official – Build now
10 sources · corrected

Amazon's Selling Partner API lets developers and sellers programmatically manage Amazon seller and vendor accounts, including listings, orders, inventory, fulfillment, reports and finances.

Auth: Login with Amazon (LWA) OAuth 2.0. Public apps use the authorization code flow, started from the Selling Partner Appstore or the developer's website, to get a refresh token. Private apps self-authorize in Seller Central or the Solution Provider Portal to get a refresh token, with a maximum of 10 authorizations per app. At runtime the refresh token is exchanged for an access token at api.amazon.com/auth/o2/token. Some operations are grantless or use a Restricted Data Token.

Conditions:

  • No API fees: Amazon cancelled the announced $1,400 annual developer fee and the usage fees in May 2026. Previously announced fees are not in effect.
  • Developers must register a developer profile (Seller Central or Solution Provider Portal) and Amazon reviews it. Amazon decides whether to grant or deny SP-API access based on the use cases, security controls and policy compliance (AUP, DPP, Solution Provider Portal Agreement). Registration must be completed by a primary account user.
  • Private seller apps need a professional selling account; individual accounts are not eligible. Self-authorizing a Seller Central account requires the Primary User.
  • Public apps must be approved by Amazon and listed in the Selling Partner Appstore.
  • Public app refresh tokens must be renewed annually.
  • Authorization limits apply to both public and private apps, including a maximum of 10 authorizations per application.
  • Sandbox endpoints can be called without seller authorization while the developer profile is under review.
  • Restricted roles, such as PII access, need extra approval and a Restricted Data Token.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: orders, listings and catalog items, inventory and FBA, fulfillment, reports, feeds, finances, notifications, vendor orders and shipments · MCP: https://github.com/amzn/selling-partner-agentic-toolkit

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: Public apps must use OAuth 2.0 to obtain authorization from selling partners.
    Public applications must use [OAuth 2.0](https://developer-docs.amazon.com/sp-api/docs/authorizing-selling-partner-api-applications) to obtain authorization from selling partners to call the SP-API on the selling partner's behalf. source ↗
  • auth: Refresh token is exchanged for an access token at the LWA token endpoint.
    your application sends a `POST` request to the Login with Amazon (LWA) authorization server (`https://api.amazon.com/auth/o2/token`) with the following parameters source ↗
  • access: Amazon cancelled the announced SP-API annual and usage fees, so no fees apply.
    we have decided that we will not move forward with the SP-API usage and annual fees at this time. source ↗
  • access: Amazon reviews the developer profile and decides whether to grant or deny API access.
    The decision to grant or deny Selling Partner API access is made based on the information that you provide in your Developer Profile source ↗
  • access: Public apps need Amazon approval for the Appstore.
    Public applications require Amazon approval for publishing to the Selling Partner Appstore and selling partners must authorize their use. source ↗
  • access: Private seller apps require a professional selling account.
    Note that in Seller Central, you must have a professional selling account to develop SP-API private seller applications. Individual accounts are not eligible. source ↗
  • access: Authorization limit per application.
    There is a maximum of 10 authorizations per application. source ↗

    To self-authorize a Seller Central account, you must be the Primary User of that account. source ↗
  • api: SP-API is a REST-based API with public docs.
    Welcome to Selling Partner API Documentation. Learn how to get started and build applications with Amazon's REST-based SP-API. source ↗
  • mcp: Amazon's official GitHub org publishes a Selling Partner Connector MCP server.
    The bundled `.mcp.json` registers the Amazon Selling Partner Connector (MCP server) at `https://sellingpartner-ai.amazon.com/mcp`. source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, LWA OAuth 2.0 with refresh token exchange at api.amazon.com/auth/o2/token is confirmed. Private self-authorization still yields an LWA refresh token, so oauth2 is a reasonable single method.
  • access: suspect, Evidence supports the gates (profile registration, professional account, Primary User, 10 authorizations, Appstore approval). Two things to check. (1) Amazon has historically charged an annual fee for public developers and some paid-tier fees for SP-API usage (2026 pricing changes); the record's own open question admits fees are unchecked. (2) Marking dev_access as app_review is questionable because private self-authorization and sandbox access exist, so dev access may be closer to self-serve. Researcher should verify fees and developer-registration requirements.
  • api: supported, The REST-based claim is supported. Broad resource families, the GitHub models and the sandbox are consistent with the public docs. Webhooks is loosely labelled, since SP-API notifications go through SQS/EventBridge, but this is a minor issue.
  • mcp: supported, The amzn GitHub org repo registers a hosted MCP connector at sellingpartner-ai.amazon.com/mcp with Seller Central OAuth. The source is Amazon's own org, so official is reasonable. Whether third-party agent platforms can use it is flagged in the open questions.
  • verdict: supported, build_with_conditions fits, since developer profile review and Appstore approval for public apps are named conditions, and private self-authorization is available without Appstore review. Confidence is medium. Fees should be confirmed, but the verdict is not contradicted.

  • access.production_access: app_review → free
  • verdict: build_with_conditions → build_now

Open questions

  • Amazon reviews each developer profile and can deny access. The docs don't say how often or why, so approval isn't guaranteed.
  • Sandbox data is static and its coverage of operations wasn't verified.
  • Per-role approval requirements for restricted data (PII) were not examined in detail.
  • The Appstore listing process for public apps (timeline, criteria) was not fetched in this pass.

The OAuth consent flow is documented only for the MCP endpoint. No public OAuth app model for the REST API was found, so Composio's OAuth2 support most likely rides on the MCP consent flow (not confirmed).

  • Only workspace owners and admins can create API keys
  • Rate limit of 60 requests/min by default, with dynamic throttling
  • Hosted MCP needs a Lite plan or higher, and its terms forbid use via custom scripts or standalone HTTP clients
  • API keys expire after 1 year and are invalidated if the creating user leaves the workspace

Clay is a go-to-market data enrichment and workflow platform that aggregates 200+ data vendors, with a public API, CLI and MCP server.

Auth: Public REST API uses a personal Clay API key (clay-api-key header) tied to a user and workspace, created in the app under API and CLI → API keys or via the clay CLI. The hosted MCP server uses OAuth 2.1 authorization code + PKCE with open Dynamic Client Registration. No documented OAuth for the REST API itself.

Conditions:

  • Clay docs state the developer platform (CLI and Public API) is available on all plans including free, trial and Legacy; API/CLI calls consume the same credits and actions as in-product work, with no extra fee
  • Search result caps scale by plan: Free 100 results per 30 days, Trial 10,000 per 14 days, paid self-serve 1,000,000 per 30 days; exceeding returns HTTP 402
  • Structured table queries (joins, ranges, paging past 100 rows) need API table sync, an Enterprise feature; basic row reads work on any plan
  • Public API keys are personal (tied to a Clay user and workspace), not app-level OAuth credentials
  • The current public API has no list-tables endpoint; table IDs must be known up front
  • Plugin setup requires Editor or Admin access in the Clay workspace

Public OAuth app review: no · Resources: me/workspace, credits, searches, data marketplace enrichments, functions/Claygents/workflows (routines), tables (read-only, Enterprise for structured queries) · MCP: https://api.clay.com/v3/mcp

Cloudflare is a global network and cloud platform offering CDN, DNS, security, Zero Trust and developer services (Workers, R2), all manageable through a single REST API.

Auth: Customers create scoped API tokens (user tokens or account-owned tokens) in the dashboard; legacy Global API key also exists. Since June 2026 Cloudflare also has a self-managed OAuth app model (Authorization Code flow, PKCE for public clients) where developers create OAuth clients under Manage account > OAuth clients. Clients are private by default and can be made public after domain verification. Cloudflare's MCP server also uses OAuth.

Conditions:

  • Free self-serve signup with email and password; API tokens are created in the dashboard. No official page found that states API availability per plan; the free-plan claim is inferred from free signup plus token creation, so it is not confirmed for every endpoint.
  • Some endpoints and features depend on the plan or product; this was not verified.
  • Account-owned tokens are not compatible with every endpoint (see compatibility matrix).
  • Token permissions must be scoped by the customer to the zones or accounts the integration needs.
  • OAuth clients are private by default (only members of the parent account can authorize). To serve any Cloudflare user, the client must be made public, which requires domain verification of the client URL.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: zones, dns, workers, r2, zero trust, accounts and iam, oauth clients · MCP: https://github.com/cloudflare/mcp

Auth: Management API: personal access token (scoped or classic) for your own account, or OAuth2 for third-party apps acting on behalf of a Supabase user. Project data API: publishable key (sb_publishable_...) or secret key (sb_secret_...), created in Dashboard Settings > API Keys.

  • Management API rate limit is 120 requests per minute per user, per project or organization scope, per endpoint, with stricter limits on some endpoints
  • Resources created via the API are billed at normal pricing

Neo4j is a graph database platform (self-managed or Aura cloud) queried with Cypher, with a cloud management API and per-database HTTP Query API and drivers.

Auth: Aura API: client ID/secret created in the Aura Console (Client credentials page) are exchanged for a 1-hour Bearer token via the OAuth 2.0 client_credentials grant at api.neo4j.io/oauth/token (token endpoint uses HTTP Basic with client ID/secret). This is a machine-to-machine flow, not a third-party multi-tenant authorization-code app model. Database access (Query API/Bolt) uses database username/password, or SSO bearer tokens on Enterprise/Aura.

Conditions:

  • Aura API credentials can be created on all Aura tiers
  • Aura API rate limit is 25 req/min for Free and Pro trial without billing, 125 req/min for paid tiers
  • Billing endpoints limited to 10 requests/day per organization
  • Query API disabled by default on self-managed versions < 5.25 and needs enabling
  • Each customer must supply their own instance URI and credentials

Public OAuth app review: not_applicable · Resources: instances, snapshots, projects/tenants, customer-managed keys, graph analytics sessions, Cypher queries (Query API) · MCP: https://github.com/neo4j/mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • auth: Aura API uses OAuth 2.0, so oauth2 belongs in the auth methods.
    The Aura API uses OAuth 2.0 for API authentication. source ↗
  • auth: Token endpoint is authenticated with client ID/secret via HTTP Basic.
    Authentication to the token endpoint uses HTTP Basic Authentication, where the client ID and client secret are provided as the username and password, respectively. source ↗
  • auth: Token request uses the client_credentials grant.
    | `grant_type` | `client_credentials` | source ↗
  • access: Any Aura tier can create API credentials.
    All tiers can create Aura API credentials. source ↗
  • access: Free and trial accounts without billing are limited to 25 requests per minute.
    AuraDB Free and AuraDB Professional trial accounts without a billing method attached, get a rate limit of 25 requests per minute. source ↗
  • api: Aura API base URL.
    The base URL for the Aura API is `https://api.neo4j.io`. source ↗
  • api: Query API runs Cypher over HTTP.
    The Query API allows to execute Cypher statements against a Neo4j server through HTTP requests. source ↗
  • mcp: Official Neo4j MCP server in the neo4j GitHub org.
    Neo4j MCP gives AI assistants and LLM-powered tools direct, structured access to your Neo4j graph database. source ↗

Critic (L3)

Auth: Service accounts are recommended. They use an OAuth 2.0 client-credentials flow: a client ID and secret are exchanged for 1-hour access tokens. Legacy programmatic API keys (public and private key) use HTTP Digest. An Organization Owner creates either one. Atlas can require an IP access list for API calls. No per-customer authorization-code OAuth flow is documented for the Admin API; delegated app access exists for partner apps and MCP clients.

Conditions:

  • Service accounts belong to exactly one organization, so each customer org needs its own credentials

9 sources · corrected

Datadog is a cloud observability and security platform for metrics, logs, traces, monitors, dashboards and incidents, with a broad public API.

Auth: Customer's own account: org-level API key (writes/submits data) plus an application key (reads; tied to a user or service account, optionally scoped), both created in Organization Settings. Newer standalone Personal Access Tokens (ddpat_) and Service Access Tokens (ddsat_) need no API key pairing. OAuth2 authorization code (with PKCE) exists, but only for approved Technology Partners building official integrations via the Developer Platform; standalone OAuth clients are not supported. Requests must go to the customer's Datadog site. The hosted MCP server uses OAuth.

Conditions:

  • Developer needs a Datadog account; the pricing page offers a free trial, after which you can move to a Free tier (up to five Basic hosts, 1-day metric retention) or a paid plan.
  • Not verified in docs: whether the Free tier (post-trial) includes API/application key access or which endpoints need paid products (e.g. logs, security, incident management).
  • Customers create their own API key and application key (or PAT) in Organization Settings, which requires appropriate permissions.
  • OAuth2 is limited to approved Technology Partners via the Datadog Partner Network and Developer Platform; OAuth can only be tested in a partner developer organization until Datadog publishes the integration. Standalone OAuth clients are not supported.
  • Customers must supply their Datadog site, because the API host differs per site.
  • Some APIs (App Builder, Actions Connections, Workflow Automation) need Actions API access enabled on the application key.
  • Endpoint rate limits vary and can be raised through Datadog support.
  • The hosted MCP server is not GovCloud compatible.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: metrics, logs, events, monitors, dashboards, incidents, users, key management, synthetics, security monitoring, service level objectives · MCP: https://mcp.datadoghq.com/v1/mcp

Auth: OAuth2 authorization code flow for multi-user integrations (register at airtable.com/create/oauth); personal access tokens (airtable.com/create/tokens, or service-account tokens for Enterprise) for single-account use. Legacy API keys were deprecated in Feb 2024.

Conditions:

  • Any Airtable user can register an OAuth integration or create a personal access token; the core API is not restricted to a paid tier
  • Monthly API call quotas are plan-gated: Free plan 1000 calls per month, Team plan 100000 per month (Business and Enterprise Scale effectively unlimited per Airtable's plan table).   • Rate limit is 5 requests/second per base on all plans, and 50 requests/second per user or service account for PATs; Airtable may change limits or tier them by plan
  • OAuth integration needs a privacy policy URL and terms of service URL before other users can authorize it (no vendor review step found in the docs)
  • Enterprise-only endpoints (org management, permissions, collaborators, audit logs, SCIM) are labeled Enterprise in the API docs navigation; service-account tokens are Enterprise as well
  • MCP server: orgs that do not want Dynamic Client Registration can have admins approve a manually created OAuth app in the admin panel (optional path; DCR works by default)
  • Token access is bounded by granted scopes, granted bases, and the granting user's own permission level

74Jira
Productivity and Project Management
 oauth2 api_key service_account_or_jwt free
prod: free
 rest, webhooks, sdk
broad
 official ✓ jira Build now
14 sources

Jira Cloud is Atlassian's issue and project tracking product, with a REST API for issues, projects, users, workflows and more.

Auth: OAuth 2.0 authorization code (3LO) apps created in the developer console are the recommended route for multi-site integrations, with rotating refresh tokens (offline_access scope). Basic auth with account email plus API token works for scripts but Atlassian discourages apps from collecting API tokens. Forge (OAuth) and Connect (JWT) apps are also supported.

Conditions:

  • Atlassian says apps that collect API tokens or tell customers to create their own 3LO apps don't comply with its security requirements, so API tokens are not a viable route for a multi-tenant product. A single distributable 3LO app is the recommended route.
  • A 3LO app is private by default; sharing must be enabled in the developer console Distribution settings, and the authorization link is sent to users.
  • Shared 3LO apps show users a warning that Atlassian hasn't reviewed them until the integration is approved. Approval goes through the Marketplace listing process, and an informative Marketplace listing is not required to submit. The review is not a hard block, but its timeline is unknown.
  • Rate limits: points-based hourly quota (65,000 points/hour in the default shared Global Pool across all tenants), per-second burst limits per endpoint, and per-issue write limits. These are enforced for 3LO apps from March 2, 2026. HTTP 429 with Retry-After is returned.
  • Rotating refresh tokens expire after 90 days of inactivity, so users must re-authorize.
  • Apps can only do what the authenticated user's Jira permissions allow.
  • Developers can get a free cloud development site via the Cloud Developer Bundle. Whether the REST API is available on every customer plan (including Free) was not confirmed in official docs; community answers say most endpoints work on free sites.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: issues, projects, users, groups, workflows, dashboards, attachments, comments, filters, permissions, project roles · MCP: https://mcp.atlassian.com/v2/mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

  • mcp: The MCP server uses OAuth 2.1 or accepts an API token for headless auth.
    The server initiates a secure OAuth 2.1 authorization flow (or accepts an API token for headless authentication). source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • auth: supported, Evidence supports basic auth with email+API token (api_key), OAuth 2.0 3LO, and Forge/Connect (JWT). Methods fit the rubric.
  • access: suspect, The cited pages support the gates (3LO warning until reviewed, permissions, discouraged API tokens). But 'free' production access is not backed by pricing evidence (the record admits it didn't check free plan API access). Also, Atlassian's statement that apps collecting API tokens violate security requirements may make api_key a non-viable route for a multi-tenant product. Check the Marketplace approval requirements and whether a review is needed before a shared 3LO app can be used without the warning.
  • api: supported, REST v3 URLs and ADF support are confirmed by the quoted pages. Breadth is plausible. The webhooks and sdk types are not shown in the evidence but are well known.
  • mcp: supported, The Atlassian-hosted Rovo MCP page confirms the official server, the endpoint, and OAuth 2.1 or an API token for headless use. Rovo credit costs remain unchecked but that doesn't contradict the status.
  • verdict: suspect, build_now is plausible but confidence is 'high' while open questions remain: free plan API access, Marketplace approval timeline, and quotas. Also the blocker field is the literal string '""', which is a data error. Coda is a collaborative doc platform that combines documents, tables and automations, and it has a public REST API for docs, pages, tables and rows.

Conditions:

  • Rate limits are per user: reads 100 requests per 6s, writes 10 per 6s, doc content writes 5 per 10s, listing docs 4 per 6s.
  • The MCP server is in beta. Free-plan Doc Makers get only a limited 'free taste' of MCP requests (30 per week, 60 per month), and editors on free plans are limited to read tools.
  • No OAuth app model found for the REST API, so there is no public app review. Tokens are per user.

  • Agent-tagged restricted keys are subject to approval rules requiring a designated reviewer for sensitive actions
  • MCP OAuth refresh tokens expire after 14 days; MCP OAuth is not supported for connected accounts

Public OAuth app review: no · Resources: customers, payment_intents, charges, refunds, subscriptions, invoices, products/prices, payouts/balance, Connect accounts, Treasury, Identity, disputes, webhook endpoints · MCP: https://mcp.stripe.com

86QuickBooks
Finance and Fintech
 oauth2 free
prod: app_review
 rest, graphql, sdk, webhooks
broad
 official ✓ quickbooks Build with conditions
Free sandbox and a documented API are available, but production keys need Intuit's app assessment questionnaire and approval plus an annual security review. Under the Intuit App Partner Program, the free Builder tier caps data-read (CorePlus) calls at 500,000 per month and blocks calls above that. Heavier apps need a paid Silver or higher tier.
14 sources

QuickBooks Online is Intuit's cloud accounting software, and its developer platform exposes accounting data (invoices, customers, bills, reports and so on) through an OAuth 2.0-secured API.

Auth: OAuth 2.0 authorization code flow. Create an app in the Intuit Developer Portal to get Client ID/Secret. Users authorize per QuickBooks company (realmId). Access tokens last 60 minutes and refresh tokens are rotated. Separate Development (sandbox) and Production keys.

Conditions:

  • Production keys require completing Intuit's app assessment questionnaire, which Intuit reviews before approval. This applies to private, unlisted apps too.
  • Annual security assessment for all apps on the platform.
  • Intuit App Partner Program (US launch July 2025; UK/AU/CA from Nov 2025): every app is auto-enrolled at the Builder tier, which has no fee but is capped at 500,000 CorePlus (data-read) API credits per month; calls above the cap are blocked, not billed. Silver/Gold/Platinum charge a monthly program fee plus variable CorePlus overage fees.
  • Core (create/update) API calls are unmetered and uncharged on all tiers. CorePlus (reads, queries, reports) are metered. Apps that read heavily may need a paid tier.
  • Builder-tier apps listed on the Intuit App Marketplace are subject to delisting unless they subscribe to Silver. Some premium APIs (e.g. Workforce API) are available only to Silver and above.
  • Production OAuth redirect URIs must be public HTTPS (localhost is rejected).
  • Development keys work only with sandbox companies; production keys are separate.
  • Some features depend on the customer's QBO tier (Simple Start/Essentials/Plus/Advanced) or settings such as multi-currency.
  • Payments API and regulated industries have extra requirements.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: customers, vendors, invoices, bills, payments, accounts, items, journal entries, estimates, reports · MCP: https://github.com/intuit/quickbooks-online-mcp-server

Also, since 2025 Intuit has been moving to tiered pricing for API calls (Intuit App Partner Program with CorePlus/Builder tiers and metered call charges). 87Xero

 official ✓ xero Build with conditions
The free Starter tier allows only 5 connected organisations. A multi-tenant integration needs a paid tier (Core at $35 AUD/month for 50 connections, Plus for 1,000 connections, which requires App Certification). Each organisation can connect only 5 uncertified apps.
11 sources · audited · corrected

Xero is cloud accounting software for small businesses and their advisors, with a broad REST API for accounting, payroll, files, assets and more.

Auth: OAuth 2.0 authorization code flow or PKCE for multi-organisation apps (developer registers an app at developer.xero.com). Custom Connections use the client_credentials grant for a single organisation (paid, UK/AU/NZ/US orgs only).

Conditions:

  • Starter tier is free (no charge, no payment method) but capped at 5 connections (tenant organisations) and 1,000 API calls per day per org. Enough for development and testing, not for serving many customers.
  • Serving more than 5 customer organisations requires a paid tier with a card on file: Core ($35 AUD/month, 50 connections), Plus ($245 AUD/month, 1,000 connections, requires App Certification), Advanced ($1,445 AUD/month, 10,000 connections, requires certification and security assessment), Enterprise (price on application).
  • Paid tiers also include egress allotments (Core 10 GB, Plus 50 GB, Advanced 250 GB) with overage at $2.40 AUD per GB.
  • Each Xero organisation can connect a maximum of five uncertified apps; there is no limit on certified apps, so broad distribution realistically needs App Certification.
  • Xero App Store listing needs Plus or above and is not available on Starter or Core.
  • Journals endpoint, Practice Manager (XPM) API and Bulk Connections need Advanced or above plus a security assessment and use-case approval.
  • The hosted Remote MCP server is Demo org only on Starter.
  • Custom Connections (client_credentials) cost a monthly fee on the Xero organisation, connect to one organisation only, and are available only for UK, AU, NZ and US orgs.
  • Some scopes are being deprecated for granular scopes; Custom Connections created from Apr 29, 2026 need the granular scopes.

Public OAuth app review: yes · Resources: invoices, credit notes, contacts, bank transactions, manual journals, accounts, payments, payroll, files, assets, projects, bank feeds, practice manager · MCP: https://github.com/XeroAPI/xero-mcp-server

Auth: REST API: static API key sent as 'Authorization: PB-Token {API_KEY}'. PitchBook provisions the key; there is no OAuth and no self-serve key generation. API credits must also be purchased, because each call has a credit cost. MCP connectors: PitchBook's help pages describe SSO sign-in for licensed users. The exact MCP auth protocol (e.g. OAuth) is not documented on a vendor page I fetched.

  • Access starts with a request to the Direct Data team; there is no self-serve signup

13 sources · corrected

Higgsfield is a generative image and video platform with a pay-as-you-go REST API, official SDKs, a CLI and a hosted MCP server.

Auth: Developer REST API: credentials (key ID + secret) are created in the Higgsfield Console and sent as 'Authorization: Key ID:SECRET'. The official MCP server (mcp.higgsfield.ai/mcp) and the CLI ('higgsfield auth login') use browser sign-in to the user's Higgsfield account instead of API keys.

Conditions:

  • REST API is pay-as-you-go: you top up a balance and pay per generation. The docs I fetched (FAQ, billing, pricing) mention no free tier, trial credits or minimum top-up, so a developer needs a funded balance to test. This is not confirmed absent, since the Console itself was not viewable.
  • Funds added to the balance expire one year after they are added
  • MCP/CLI route requires an active paid Higgsfield subscription, and every generation deducts credits
  • Via MCP, unlimited-model access and free generations are not available
  • Invoice billing is only for committed-use/enterprise contracts, which go through sales
  • Individual models may have separate access restrictions and can return 404, 423 or 503
  • Rate limits depend on account and model

Public OAuth app review: not_applicable · Resources: generation (model endpoints for image and video), requests (status, cancel, delete), cost estimate, media upload, webhooks · MCP: https://mcp.higgsfield.ai/mcp

Mermaid CLI (mmdc) is an open-source local command-line tool that renders mermaid diagram definitions, or mermaid blocks in Markdown, to SVG, PNG or PDF.

Auth: No authentication. It is a local npm package or Docker image that runs a headless Chromium via Puppeteer. There are no accounts, keys or remote service.

Conditions:

  • Runs locally and needs Node.js, or Docker/Podman, plus headless Chromium via Puppeteer
  • Known Linux sandbox and Docker permission issues may need a Puppeteer config such as --no-sandbox
  • There is no hosted API, so Composio would have to run it in a sandboxed execution environment

Public OAuth app review: not_applicable · Resources: diagram rendering (mermaid definition to SVG/PNG/PDF), markdown files with embedded mermaid blocks · MCP: https://github.com/andrewginns/mermaid-mcp-server

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

    It takes a mermaid definition file as input and generates an SVG/PNG/PDF file as output. source ↗
  • auth: It is installed as a local npm package. No account, key or remote service is involved, so there is no auth.
    npm install -g @mermaid-js/mermaid-cli source ↗
  • access: Free to obtain as an npm package or Docker image. No signup is needed and there are no plan tiers.
    docker pull minlag/mermaid-cli source ↗

Critic (L3)

  • mcp: supported, The andrewginns repo is a third-party Python wrapper over mermaid-cli, so 'community' is correct. The note about the official mcp.mermaid.ai server is explicitly uncited and applies to the separate Mermaid Chart product, so it does not affect this field.

That is closer to needs_research, or build_with_conditions with the condition 'requires sandboxed execution with Node and Chromium'. Check whether 'limited' is the right label under the rubric.

TranscriptAPI is a hosted REST API and MCP server that returns YouTube transcripts, video search, and channel and playlist data.

Auth: The REST API takes an API key created in the dashboard and sent as a Bearer token. The hosted MCP server also accepts OAuth 2.1, but that is for MCP clients. For a direct toolkit, each end user supplies their own API key.

Conditions:

  • New accounts get 100 free credits with no card required.
  • There is one plan, about $5 a month for 1,000 credits, or $54 a year. API and MCP access are included in it, and extra credits are sold as top-ups. This is credit metering, not a higher tier or add-on gate.
  • The API returns HTTP 402 when credits run out or there is no active subscription. Top-up credits need an active subscription.
  • Every call needs an API key plus at least one credit. Channel latest, channel resolve and info are free of credit cost.
  • Rate limits depend on billing cycle (200 requests per minute on monthly, 300 on annual) and are shared across all keys on the account.

Public OAuth app review: not_applicable · Resources: transcripts, video info, search, channels, playlists · MCP: https://transcriptapi.com/mcp

Evidence (every quote re-checked on its page)

    When credits are exhausted, the API returns `HTTP 402 Payment Required`. source ↗
  • access: Rate limits are shared across all keys on an account.
    The limit applies **per account**: all API keys on your account share it. source ↗
  • api: The REST API covers transcripts, search, channels and playlists, with paginated playlist videos at 1 credit per page.

Free) generated in Grain settings; Workspace Access Token (admin, Business/Enterprise) for workspace-wide access; OAuth2 authorization code + PKCE for multi-user apps (client_id/secret requested manually from Grain, or Client ID Metadata Document URL for public clients).   • Personal Access Token available on every plan including Free

  • OAuth2 client_id/secret for server-side apps obtained manually from Grain; workspace scope only for clients verified by Grain
  • No sandbox; test against live workspace
  • Rate limit 300 requests/min per token
  • MCP deals and coaching tools are Business/Enterprise only

  • The hosted MCP server needs clients that support DCR (dynamic client registration)
  • Developer-portal docs do not state an app review or marketplace requirement for production OAuth apps; this was not verified

Public OAuth app review: unknown · Resources: scheduling / booking, event types, scheduled events, invitees, availability, routing forms, users and organizations, webhooks, contacts, notetaker, activity log, data compliance · MCP: https://mcp.calendly.com

confidence medium · 6 tool calls · 27s · researched 2026-11-24T03:07 · added 2026-11-24, rule/quote flags 0, not human audited, not counted in the study's statistics

Run it on any app

The live page is static (no API key is exposed). The same agent runs locally on any app outside this list in about 30–60 seconds. It needs Node 22, a Composio API key, and Claude Code login or ANTHROPIC_API_KEY.

git clone https://github.com/Himanshu-4685/composio-app-research && cd composio-app-research && npm install
cp .env.example .env            # add COMPOSIO_API_KEY
npm run research -- --app "Calendly" --hint calendly.com    # any app; prints the full record
npm run research -- --app "Calendly" --hint calendly.com --add   # also add it to this page's list
npm run ui                       # local page with a "Research any app" box (+ "Add to list" checkbox)
npm run render -- --run baseline-v1   # rebuild this page from saved data (no network)

New-app results get automatic verification only (rules + quote check + critic); they are not human audited.
